# AgentifyMe AI Agent Implementation Plan — DKMS Durable Knowledge Management Service

**Date:** 2026-10-03  
**Status:** V2 — user decisions applied; ready for Hermes execution  
**Primary executor:** Hermes / STEA-004 running GLM 5.3 Flash  
**Implementation worker:** Claude Code skill using Claude Opus 5.5, medium reasoning  
**Primary objective:** Create and integrate an isolated **Durable Knowledge Management Service (DKMS)** so valuable agent knowledge is no longer treated as an ACMS-owned durable-memory responsibility.

---

# 0. Mission

Build the first production-usable DKMS slice.

The service must preserve **useful, durable, attributable knowledge** produced by agents and the systems around them without trying to copy every byte of every system into one database.

The most valuable first knowledge classes are:

- approved AI-agent instruction plans;
- final handoffs and durable summaries of handoffs;
- architecture decisions and ADR references;
- Jira scope snapshots and completion summaries;
- work/project trajectory summaries;
- failure signatures, recovery attempts, root-cause findings, side effects, and lessons learned;
- successful remediation/playbook knowledge;
- model/agent evaluation findings that explain why work succeeded or failed;
- selected agent knowledge that should survive runtime/session replacement;
- curated records that may later become training/evaluation data.

The DKMS is **not** a replacement for Jira, GitHub, ACMS, Server Manager, LLM Manager, PDU Manager, Hermes state backup, or service-specific telemetry.

The DKMS is the durable knowledge layer that those systems can write to and retrieve from.

---

# 0.1 User decisions applied in V2

[x] Canonical repository name: `startupteams/dkms-project-framework`.

[x] Full approved plans/handoffs/raw source default retention: **90 days**; durable summaries/trajectory knowledge retained indefinitely.

[x] ACMS keeps the bulk of raw operational conversation/session information; DKMS stores selected high-value knowledge and summaries rather than all raw data.

[x] Registered STEA agents may write useful knowledge/lessons directly to DKMS outside an ACMS Work Item through scoped MCP.

[x] **Validated internal failure/recovery lessons may be automatically training-approved.** The system must distinguish automatic approval from human approval and record whether human review occurred.

[x] DKMS gets a **new dedicated VM** and a **dedicated Kubernetes service**. It does not run inside the ACMS container.

[x] ACMS must support agents/operators logging **Lessons Learned** to DKMS.

[x] DKMS is **not primarily a Work-statistics database**. ACMS owns detailed Work statistics/status. DKMS stores lessons, architecture/implementation trajectory, selected handoff knowledge, and concise dated Jira/work summaries useful for understanding what changed and what work may logically come next.

[x] The next ~15 instruction files remain predominantly internal reliability/security/evaluation/infrastructure work before serious external Speed-to-Lead expansion.

---

# 1. Source precedence

Use the following sources in this order:

1. `2026-10-03-commented-agentifyme-architecture-workbook.md`
   - current human architecture direction;
   - especially MEM, RET, REC, RUN, WORK, and the user's DKMS comments.
2. `HANDOFF-20261003-AGENTIFYME-JIRA-BUSINESS-IDEA-WORKFLOW.md`
   - latest live implementation proof and current gaps.
3. `2026-10-03-agentifyme-ai-agent-implementation-plan.md`
   - prior execution-plan conventions and acceptance discipline.
4. Current production code/configuration and current repository state.
5. Older June 2026 research files only as **historical intent**, not as current infrastructure truth.
6. Existing ADRs and repository documentation.

When a live system contradicts an old document, record the discrepancy. Do not silently rewrite history.

---

# 2. Current verified starting point

The October 3 handoff reports that the real Jira-to-outcome workflow is substantially proven:

- STNA-90 was cloned from live STNA-86 and successfully reconciled into ACMS.
- One Work Item and assignment were created and completed.
- Jira handoff posting and intake idempotency were proven.
- The business-idea output and one-way repository sync were proven.
- The issue reached IN REVIEW.
- No production ACMS, gateway, or Server Manager code changed during that final workflow proof.

The same handoff identifies remaining gaps that **must not be misreported as complete**:

- nested Claude Code chat/session visibility in the ACMS UI remains incomplete;
- live steer/pause/resume/cancel proof remains incomplete;
- the daily business-idea scheduler is not yet registered;
- failure-injection/rollback acceptance is partial;
- STNA-89 remains a separate human-gated item;
- VM119 qga / registry-monitoring credential work remains open;
- internal MCP DNS/TLS remains open;
- Jira gateway mutation remains a policy decision;
- ADR-0023 remains Proposed;
- Vercel Git integration remains disconnected.

These gaps are not the main DKMS objective. Do not let them consume the DKMS implementation window unless they directly block DKMS.

---

# 3. Superseding architecture decision: ACMS is not the durable knowledge database

Treat this as the new working split:

```text
Human / approved plans
        |
        v
Jira ----------------------------+
| authoritative scoped work      |
| when a Jira issue exists       |
+--------------------------------+
        |
        v
ACMS
| Work / assignment / state / controls / operational events
| recent chat and streaming work notes
| references to durable knowledge
        |
        +-----------------------> DKMS
                                  | approved plans
                                  | handoff source + summary
                                  | ADR / architecture trajectory
                                  | Jira scope/completion snapshots
                                  | failure/recovery learning
                                  | evaluation findings
                                  | agent lessons
                                  | training candidates
                                  | context-pack source records
                                  |
                                  +--> MCP namespace: dkms.*
                                  +--> Markdown export
                                  +--> future training/eval export

Server Manager / LLM Manager / PDU / Hermes / GitHub
        |
        +-----------------------> selected high-value facts and references
```

## 3.1 ACMS continues to own

- Work Item identity and status;
- assignments;
- durable Agent UID relationships;
- operational control state;
- recent execution/session events;
- recent chat/streaming notes needed for operating current work;
- attention and policy workflow;
- work/project/product relationships;
- links to DKMS knowledge records.

## 3.2 DKMS owns

- durable knowledge objects and their versions;
- provenance and source references;
- durable summaries;
- failure-learning records;
- architecture/decision trajectory;
- selected requirement/scope snapshots;
- selected agent lessons;
- training/evaluation eligibility metadata;
- retrieval/context-pack inputs;
- human-readable Markdown exports.

## 3.3 Other systems remain authoritative for their domains

- **Jira:** human-scoped work and externally visible result/status when a Jira issue exists.
- **GitHub:** source code, PRs, commits, repository-native docs.
- **Server Manager:** runtime placement/lifecycle and runtime-recovery execution.
- **LLM Manager:** model availability/routing/usage/benchmark facts.
- **PDU Manager:** physical-power authority.
- **Hermes profile/state repository:** complete harness/profile state until a future state architecture replaces it.
- **DKMS:** curated durable knowledge derived from and linked to these systems.

Do not copy entire source systems into DKMS merely because an API exists.

---

# 4. Executor contract

## 4.1 Hermes / GLM 5.3 Flash

Hermes is the orchestrator and tracker.

Hermes must:

- read this plan and the latest handoff before changing code;
- maintain the objective and acceptance matrix;
- use the Claude Code skill for bounded implementation work;
- keep one implementation writer per branch;
- checkpoint between major phases;
- persist progress before `/new`, model limits, or context exhaustion;
- track Jira / Work / repository / PR / deployment / DKMS record IDs;
- provide a final Markdown handoff.

Hermes should not become the primary coder merely because a change is small if the plan expects Claude Code implementation.

## 4.2 Claude Code / Opus 5.5 medium

The requested implementation model is **Claude Opus 5.5, medium reasoning**.

At the start of the run:

1. record the requested model;
2. record the effective model actually returned by Claude Code;
3. if Opus 5.5 is unavailable, do not silently substitute another paid API/model;
4. checkpoint the exact limitation and ask for a model-policy decision unless the existing approved configuration explicitly defines a fallback.

Each Claude packet must contain:

- Work UID and Jira key;
- repository and branch;
- objective;
- allowed scope;
- current phase;
- acceptance criteria;
- known constraints;
- rollback requirement;
- source pointers;
- **no secret values**.

---

# 5. Security rule: secrets are not knowledge

DKMS must never become a secret dumping ground.

Do not ingest:

- passwords;
- API keys;
- bearer tokens;
- cookies;
- private keys;
- raw credential files;
- environment files containing secrets;
- screenshots or logs that expose secrets.

Knowledge may contain a **secret reference**, credential name, secret-store path, or statement that a credential exists, but not the secret value.

Before a record becomes training-eligible, it must pass secret/sensitive-data checks.

If ingestion detects likely secret material:

```text
reject or quarantine
→ log metadata only
→ mark training_eligibility = deny
→ provide a sanitized-ingestion path
```

Do not echo any secret values from historical source files into Jira, DKMS, logs, prompts, handoffs, screenshots, or Git.

---

# 6. DKMS MVP architecture

## 6.1 Repository

**User decision:** the canonical repository name is approved as:

`startupteams/dkms-project-framework`

At execution start, verify that this exact repository does not already exist. If absent, create **this exact repository** under the Startup Teams organization; do not create a differently named substitute.

Initialize it using the Startup Teams project-framework conventions with at minimum:

- `README.md`;
- architecture/service-boundary documentation;
- requirements;
- future work / technical debt;
- ADR directory;
- deployment/runbook documentation;
- tests and CI structure appropriate to the chosen implementation.

Repository creation is part of P0/P1 acceptance, not a future naming decision.

Use the existing Startup Teams project-framework documentation conventions where practical.

## 6.2 Service boundary

DKMS must be independently deployable from ACMS.

Default MVP:

```text
DKMS service
  Python / FastAPI
  Async SQLAlchemy
  PostgreSQL
  migration-managed schema
  internal REST API
  Markdown import/export
  exact/metadata/full-text retrieval
  audit events
```

Avoid a vector database in the MVP. The current architecture direction explicitly defers vector/semantic indexing until metadata/exact/full-text retrieval proves inadequate.

## 6.3 Hosting and Kubernetes deployment

**User decision:** DKMS gets a **new dedicated virtual machine** and a **dedicated Kubernetes service**. It does **not** belong inside the ACMS container.

MVP deployment direction:

```text
new dedicated DKMS VM
  -> lightweight Kubernetes distribution (prefer current standard; K3s is acceptable if no standard exists)
      -> dedicated `dkms` namespace
          -> DKMS application Deployment/Service
          -> PostgreSQL StatefulSet or equivalently durable Kubernetes-managed database deployment
          -> PersistentVolume-backed data
          -> migration job
          -> backup/export jobs
```

Requirements:

- provision the VM through Server Manager/current placement policy;
- do not hardcode a Proxmox node unless live placement policy requires it;
- do not place by bypassing protected-host rules;
- do not change hostnames;
- use a dedicated service account/namespace and least-privilege Kubernetes RBAC;
- internal LAN only; no public ingress;
- proposed internal service name: `dkms.miam.home.arpa`;
- register the VM/service in registry/monitoring when available;
- cover the VM with PBS according to current backup policy;
- add logical PostgreSQL backup/export in addition to VM backup;
- keep Kubernetes manifests/Helm/Kustomize configuration in Git so the service can be recreated;
- design the manifests so they can move to a multi-node cluster later without making multi-node Kubernetes a prerequisite now.

This is an explicit **DKMS-specific exception** to the broader architecture's previous “defer Kubernetes until scale pain” posture. It does not automatically migrate ACMS, Server Manager, LLM Manager, or the agent fleet to Kubernetes.

## 6.4 MCP access

Add a DKMS adapter/domain to the existing internal MCP gateway rather than creating an unrelated control plane.

Namespace:

`dkms.*`

Initial tools should be narrow and schema-validated.

Suggested read tools:

```text
dkms.get
dkms.search
dkms.list_recent
dkms.list_related
dkms.context_pack
```

Suggested safe-write tools:

```text
dkms.ingest_plan
dkms.ingest_handoff
dkms.record_timeline_entry
dkms.record_failure
dkms.record_recovery_attempt
dkms.record_lesson
dkms.record_decision
dkms.mark_training_eligibility
```

`dkms.record_lesson` must support both ACMS-dispatched agents and registered STEA agents operating outside an ACMS Work Item, provided identity/provenance/scope are recorded.

Administrative/destructive tools should not be introduced in the first slice unless required for retention testing.

Deletion should normally be retention-policy driven or explicit administrative action with audit.

---

# 7. Core data model

Use a small, explicit schema rather than a generic untyped blob store.

## 7.1 `knowledge_record`

Minimum fields:

```text
knowledge_uid
record_type
title
body_markdown / normalized text
content_sha256
source_kind
source_uri
source_uid
source_version
source_created_at
project_uid
product_uid
work_uid
jira_key
agent_uid
session_uid
repository
commit_sha
authority_class
confidence
sensitivity_class
training_eligibility
training_approval_mode
training_review_state
training_eligibility_reason
retention_class
created_at
updated_at
supersedes_uid
status
tags
```

Recommended `record_type` values for MVP:

```text
execution_plan
handoff_source
handoff_summary
architecture_decision
requirement_snapshot
implementation_timeline_entry
jira_scope_summary
jira_completion_summary
failure_event
recovery_attempt
lesson_learned
runbook
evaluation_finding
agent_knowledge
training_candidate
```

`implementation_timeline_entry` is intentionally a **short knowledge/timeline record**, not a copy of ACMS Work statistics. ACMS remains authoritative for detailed Work status, timing, assignments, and operational metrics.

Do not make every type mandatory in the first migration. Implement the types required by live acceptance first.

## 7.2 Authority / epistemic state

A record must distinguish what kind of claim it contains.

Suggested values:

```text
HUMAN_APPROVED
SYSTEM_OBSERVED
AGENT_INFERRED
EXTERNAL_RETRIEVED
DERIVED_SUMMARY
VALIDATED_LESSON
```

An agent inference must not silently become a human decision.

## 7.3 Knowledge links

Support typed relationships such as:

```text
derived_from
supersedes
related_to
applies_to
result_of
failed_because
fixed_by
validated_by
summarizes
```

This supports trajectory without inventing a complex graph database.

---

# 8. Failure learning is a first-class DKMS feature

The architecture workbook explicitly identifies failure/recovery knowledge as high value and potentially useful for future model training.

For each important failure, preserve:

```text
failure signature
failure class
system/service
Work/Jira/agent/session correlation
symptoms
observed evidence
attempted method
attempt number
result
side effects
time to recovery
root cause
final fix
preventive action
rollback used
confidence
validation status
related runbook/playbook
```

## 8.1 Learning lifecycle

Do not let a model promote one guess into permanent truth.

Use:

```text
OBSERVED
→ ANALYZED
→ LESSON_PROPOSED
→ VALIDATED
→ PLAYBOOK_PROMOTED
```

A validated lesson may update a recovery playbook.

The old evidence remains linked and immutable.

## 8.2 Retry learning

The existing direction is:

```text
runtime recovery: up to 5 attempts before Human Attention
failed work reassignment: up to 3 attempts before Human Attention
```

Before retrying failed work, prefer changing in this order:

```text
1. input: instructions / context / implementation strategy
2. brain: model
3. worker: agent
4. human clarification
```

DKMS should make previous attempts retrievable so retries do not blindly repeat the same unsuccessful action.

---

# 9. Ingestion policy

## 9.1 Human-approved plans

When an approved instruction plan is dispatched:

```text
dispatch Work
AND
asynchronously ingest approved plan into DKMS
```

The ingestion process must not significantly delay dispatch.

Record:

- content hash;
- source filename;
- Work/Jira relationship;
- approval provenance;
- timestamp;
- version/supersession relation.

## 9.2 Final handoffs

On Work completion or explicit handoff:

1. store or reference the source handoff;
2. create a concise durable summary;
3. correlate Jira / Work / agent / session / PR / commit / deployment / artifacts;
4. mark unresolved items;
5. link to the prior plan;
6. retain the summary indefinitely unless policy changes.

## 9.3 Jira

DKMS is **not** a Jira analytics warehouse.

For Jira-backed implementation, retain only enough durable knowledge to reconstruct the implementation trajectory:

- Jira key/title/reference;
- date the work entered execution or was materially scoped;
- short scope/acceptance summary;
- material scope-change summary when it changes architecture/implementation direction;
- date completed/reviewed;
- short outcome/what-was-implemented summary;
- links to relevant plan, handoff, PR/commit/deployment, and DKMS records.

Do **not** continuously copy Jira comments, status history, detailed Work metrics, assignee statistics, or other operational data into DKMS merely because it exists.

The goal is to understand **what changed, when it changed, and how the implementation trajectory evolved** so future agents can infer reasonable next work. Jira remains authoritative for the ticket itself.

## 9.4 ACMS

ACMS should publish knowledge-worthy events, not every heartbeat.

Candidate knowledge-worthy events:

```text
WORK_ACCEPTED              -> optional short implementation-timeline entry
SCOPE_CHANGED              -> only when material
HANDOFF_PUBLISHED          -> durable handoff + summary
WORK_REVIEWED              -> short implementation outcome/timeline entry
WORK_FAILED                -> failure-learning candidate
RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED         -> failure-learning candidate
HUMAN_ATTENTION            -> only when it creates durable lesson/context
LESSON_VALIDATED           -> durable lesson
```

**User decision:** ACMS must provide an explicit path for agents/operators to **Log Lesson Learned*

This makes it ideal for automation.\n\n### Mode 2: Interactive PTY via tmux — Multi-Turn Sessions\n\nInteractive mode gives you a full conversational REPL where you can send follow-up prompts, use slash commands, and watch Claude work in real time. **Requires tmux orchestration.**\n\n```\n# Start a tmux session\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s claude-work -x 140 -y 40\")\n\n# Launch Claude Code inside it\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'cd /path/to/project && claude' Enter\")\n\n# Wait for startup, then send your task\n# (after ~3-5 seconds for the welcome screen)\nterminal(command=\"sleep 5 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'Refactor the auth module to use JWT tokens' Enter\")\n\n# Monitor progress by capturing the pane\nterminal(command=\"sleep 15 && tmux capture-pane -t claude-work -p -S -50\")\n\n# Send follow-up tasks\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'Now add unit tests for the new JWT code' Enter\")\n\n# Exit when done\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work '/exit' Enter\")\n```\n\n**When to use interactive mode:**\n- Multi-turn iterative work (refactor → review → fix → test cycle)\n- Tasks requiring human-in-the-loop decisions\n- Exploratory coding sessions\n- When you need to use Claude's slash commands (`/compact`, `/review`, `/model`)\n\n## PTY Dialog Handling (CRITICAL for Interactive Mode)\n\nClaude Code presents up to two confirmation dialogs on first launch. You MUST handle these via tmux send-keys:\n\n### Dialog 1: Workspace Trust (first visit to a directory)\n```\n❯ 1. Yes, I trust this folder    ← DEFAULT (just press Enter)\n  2. No, exit\n```\n**Handling:** `tmux send-keys -t <session> Enter` — default selection is correct.\n\n### Dialog 2: Bypass Permissions Warning (only with --dangerously-skip-permissions)\n```\n❯ 1. No, exit                    ← DEFAULT (WRONG choice!)\n  2. Yes, I accept\n```\n**Handling:** Must navigate DOWN first, then Enter:\n```\ntmux send-keys -t <session> Down && sleep 0.3 && tmux send-keys -t <session> Enter\n```\n\n### Robust Dialog Handling Pattern\n```\n# Launch with permissions bypass\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'claude --dangerously-skip-permissions \\\"your task\\\"' Enter\")\n\n# Handle trust dialog (Enter for default \"Yes\")\nterminal(command=\"sleep 4 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Enter\")\n\n# Handle permissions dialog (Down then Enter for \"Yes, I accept\")\nterminal(command=\"sleep 3 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Down && sleep 0.3 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Enter\")\n\n# Now wait for Claude to work\nterminal(command=\"sleep 15 && tmux capture-pane -t claude-work -p -S -60\")\n```\n\n**Note:** After the first trust acceptance for a directory, the trust dialog won't appear again. Only the permissions dialog recurs each time you use `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.\n\n## CLI Subcommands\n\n| Subcommand | Purpose |\n|------------|---------|\n| `claude` | Start interactive REPL |\n| `claude \"query\"` | Start REPL with initial prompt |\n| `claude -p \"query\"` | Print mode (non-interactive, exits when done) |\n| `cat file \\| claude -p \"query\"` | Pipe content as stdin context |\n| `claude -c` | Continue the most recent conversation in this directory |\n| `claude -r \"id\"` | Resume a specific session by ID or name |\n| `claude auth login` | Sign in (add `--console` for API billing, `--sso` for Enterprise) |\n| `claude auth status` | Check login status (returns JSON; `--text` for human-readable) |\n| `claude mcp add <name> -- <cmd>` | Add an MCP server |\n| `claude mcp list` | List configured MCP servers |\n| `claude mcp remove <name>` | Remove an MCP server |\n| `claude agents` | List configured agents |\n| `claude doctor` | Run health checks on installation and auto-updater |\n| `claude update` / `claude upgrade` | Update Claude Code to latest version |\n| `claude remote-control` | Start server to control Claude from claude.ai or mobile app |\n| `claude install [target]` | Install native build (stable, latest, or specific version) |\n| `claude setup-token` | Set up long-lived auth token (requires subscription) |\n| `claude plugin` / `claude plugins` | Manage Claude Code plugins |\n| `claude auto-mode` | Inspect auto mode classifier configuration |\n\n## Print Mode Deep Dive\n\n### Structured JSON Output\n```\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Analyze auth.py for security issues' --output-format json --max-turns 5\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=120)\n```\n\nReturns a JSON object with:\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"result\",\n  \"subtype\": \"success\",\n  \"result\": \"The analysis text...\",\n  \"session_id\": \"75e2167f-...\",\n  \"num_turns\": 3,\n  \"total_cost_usd\": 0.0787,\n  \"duration_ms\": 10276,\n  \"stop_reason\": \"end_turn\",\n  \"terminal_reason\": \"completed\",\n  \"usage\": { \"input_tokens\": 5, \"output_tokens\": 603, ... },\n  \"modelUsage\": { \"claude-sonnet-4-6\": { \"costUSD\": 0.078, \"contextWindow\": 200000 } }\n}\n```\n\n**Key fields:** `session_id` for resumption, `num_turns` for agentic loop count, `total_cost_usd` for spend tracking, `subtype` for success/error detection (`success`, `error_max_turns`, `error_budget`).\n\n### Streaming JSON Output\nFor real-time token streaming, use `stream-json` with `--verbose`:\n```\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Write a summary' --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages\", timeout=60)\n```\n\nReturns newline-delimited JSON events. Filter with jq for live text:\n```\nclaude -p \"Explain X\" --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages | \\\n  jq -rj 'select(.type == \"stream_event\" and .event.delta.type? == \"text_delta\") | .event.delta.text'\n```\n\nStream events include `system/api_retry` with `attempt`, `max_retries`, and `error` fields (e.g., `rate_limit`, `billing_error`).\n\n### Bidirectional Streaming\nFor real-time input AND output streaming:\n```\nclaude -p \"task\" --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --replay-user-messages\n```\n`--replay-user-messages` re-emits user messages on stdout for acknowledgment.\n\n### Piped Input\n```\n# Pipe a file for analysis\nterminal(command=\"cat src/auth.py | claude -p 'Review this code for bugs' --max-turns 1\", timeout=60)\n\n# Pipe multiple files\nterminal(command=\"cat src/*.py | claude -p 'Find all TODO comments' --max-turns 1\", timeout=60)\n\n# Pipe command output\nterminal(command=\"git diff HEAD~3 | claude -p 'Summarize these changes' --max-turns 1\", timeout=60)\n```\n\n### JSON Schema for Structured Extraction\n```\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'List all functions in src/' --output-format json --json-schema '{\\\"type\\\":\\\"object\\\",\\\"properties\\\":{\\\"functions\\\":{\\\"type\\\":\\\"array\\\",\\\"items\\\":{\\\"type\\\":\\\"string\\\"}}},\\\"required\\\":[\\\"functions\\\"]}' --max-turns 5\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=90)\n```\n\nParse `structured_output` from the JSON result. Claude validates output against the schema before returning.\n\n### Session Continuation\n```\n# Start a task\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Start refactoring the database layer' --output-format json --max-turns 10 > /tmp/session.json\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=180)\n\n# Resume with session ID\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Continue and add connection pooling' --resume $(cat /tmp/session.json | python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)[\\\"session_id\\\"])') --max-turns 5\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=120)\n\n# Or resume the most recent session in the same directory\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'What did you do last time?' --continue --max-turns 1\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=30)\n\n# Fork a session (new ID, keeps history)\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Try a different approach' --resume <id> --fork-session --max-turns 10\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=120)\n```\n\n### Bare Mode for CI/Scripting\n```\nterminal(command=\"claude --bare -p 'Run all tests and report failures' --allowedTools 'Read,Bash' --max-turns 10\", workdir=\"/project\", timeout=180)\n```\n\n`--bare` skips hooks, plugins, MCP discovery, and CLAUDE.md loading. Fastest startup. Requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (skips OAuth).\n\nTo selectively load context in bare mode:\n| To load | Flag |\n|---------|------|\n| System prompt additions | `--append-system-prompt \"text\"` or `--append-system-prompt-file path` |\n| Settings | `--settings <file-or-json>` |\n| MCP servers | `--mcp-config <file-or-json>` |\n| Custom agents | `--agents '<json>'` |\n\n### Fallback Model for Overload\n```\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'task' --fallback-model haiku --max-turns 5\", timeout=90)\n```\nAutomatically falls back to the specified model when the default is overloaded (print mode only).\n\n## Complete CLI Flags Reference\n\n### Session & Environment\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `-p, --print` | Non-interactive one-shot mode (exits when done) |\n| `-c, --continue` | Resume most recent conversation in current directory |\n| `-r, --resume <id>` | Resume specific session by ID or name (interactive picker if no ID) |\n| `--fork-session` | When resuming, create new session ID instead of reusing original |\n| `--session-id <uuid>` | Use a specific UUID for the conversation |\n| `--no-session-persistence` | Don't save session to disk (print mode only) |\n| `--add-dir <paths...>` | Grant Claude access to additional working directories |\n| `-w, --worktree [name]` | Run in an isolated git worktree at `.claude/worktrees/<name>` |\n| `--tmux` | Create a tmux session for the worktree (requires `--worktree`) |\n| `--ide` | Auto-connect to a valid IDE on startup |\n| `--chrome` / `--no-chrome` | Enable/disable Chrome browser integration for web testing |\n| `--from-pr [number]` | Resume session linked to a specific GitHub PR |\n| `--file <specs...>` | File resources to download at startup (format: `file_id:relative_path`) |\n\n### Model & Performance\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `--model <alias>` | Model selection: `sonnet`, `opus`, `haiku`, or full name like `claude-sonnet-4-6` |\n| `--effort <level>` | Reasoning depth: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `max`, `auto` | Both |\n| `--max-turns <n>` | Limit agentic loops (print mode only; prevents runaway) |\n| `--max-budget-usd <n>` | Cap API spend in dollars (print mode only) |\n| `--fallback-model <model>` | Auto-fallback when default model is overloaded (print mode only) |\n| `--betas <betas...>` | Beta headers to include in API requests (API key users only) |\n\n### Permission & Safety\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `--dangerously-skip-permissions` | Auto-approve ALL tool use (file writes, bash, network, etc.) |\n| `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` | Enable bypass as an *option* without enabling it by default |\n| `--permission-mode <mode>` | `default`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `auto`, `dontAsk`, `bypassPermissions` |\n| `--allowedTools <tools...>` | Whitelist specific tools (comma or space-separated) |\n| `--disallowedTools <tools...>` | Blacklist specific tools |\n| `--tools <tools...>` | Override built-in tool set (`\"\"` = none, `\"default\"` = all, or tool names) |\n\n### Output & Input Format\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `--output-format <fmt>` | `text` (default), `json` (single result object), `stream-json` (newline-delimited) |\n| `--input-format <fmt>` | `text` (default) or `stream-json` (real-time streaming input) |\n| `--json-schema <schema>` | Force structured JSON output matching a schema |\n| `--verbose` | Full turn-by-turn output |\n| `--include-partial-messages` | Include partial message chunks as they arrive (stream-json + print) |\n| `--replay-user-messages` | Re-emit user messages on stdout (stream-json bidirectional) |\n\n### System Prompt & Context\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `--append-system-prompt <text>` | **Add** to the default system prompt (preserves built-in capabilities) |\n| `--append-system-prompt-file <path>` | **Add** file contents to the default system prompt |\n| `--system-prompt <text>` | **Replace** the entire system prompt (use --append instead usually) |\n| `--system-prompt-file <path>` | **Replace** the system prompt with file contents |\n| `--bare` | Skip hooks, plugins, MCP discovery, CLAUDE.md, OAuth (fastest startup) |\n| `--agents '<json>'` | Define custom subagents dynamically as JSON |\n| `--mcp-config <path>` | Load MCP servers from JSON file (repeatable) |\n| `--strict-mcp-config` | Only use MCP servers from `--mcp-config`, ignoring all other MCP configs |\n| `--settings <file-or-json>` | Load additional settings from a JSON file or inline JSON |\n| `--setting-sources <sources>` | Comma-separated sources to load: `user`, `project`, `local` |\n| `--plugin-dir <paths...>` | Load plugins from directories for this session only |\n| `--disable-slash-commands` | Disable all skills/slash commands |\n\n### Debugging\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `-d, --debug [filter]` | Enable debug logging with optional category filter (e.g., `\"api,hooks\"`, `\"!1p,!file\"`) |\n| `--debug-file <path>` | Write debug logs to file (implicitly enables debug mode) |\n\n### Agent Teams\n| Flag | Effect |\n|------|--------|\n| `--teammate-mode <mode>` | How agent teams display: `auto`, `in-process`, or `tmux` |\n| `--brief` | Enable `SendUserMessage` tool for agent-to-user communication |\n\n### Tool Name Syntax for --allowedTools / --disallowedTools\n```\nRead                    # All file reading\nEdit                    # File editing (existing files)\nWrite                   # File creation (new files)\nBash                    # All shell commands\nBash(git *)             # Only git commands\nBash(git commit *)      # Only git commit commands\nBash(npm run lint:*)    # Pattern matching with wildcards\nWebSearch               # Web search capability\nWebFetch                # Web page fetching\nmcp__<server>__<tool>   # Specific MCP tool\n```\n\n## Settings & Configuration\n\n### Settings Hierarchy (highest to lowest priority)\n1. **CLI flags** — override everything\n2. **Local project:** `.claude/settings.local.json` (personal, gitignored)\n3. **Project:** `.claude/settings.json` (shared, git-tracked)\n4. **User:** `~/.claude/settings.json` (global)\n\n### Permissions in Settings\n```json\n{\n  \"permissions\": {\n    \"allow\": [\"Bash(npm run lint:*)\", \"WebSearch\", \"Read\"],\n    \"ask\": [\"Write(*.ts)\", \"Bash(git push*)\"],\n    \"deny\": [\"Read(.env)\", \"Bash(rm -rf *)\"]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Memory Files (CLAUDE.md) Hierarchy\n1. **Global:** `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` — applies to all projects\n2. **Project:** `./CLAUDE.md` — project-specific context (git-tracked)\n3. **Local:** `.claude/CLAUDE.local.md` — personal project overrides (gitignored)\n\nUse the `#` prefix in interactive mode to quickly add to memory: `# Always use 2-space indentation`.\n\n## Interactive Session: Slash Commands\n\n### Session & Context\n| Command | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `/help` | Show all commands (including custom and MCP commands) |\n| `/compact [focus]` | Compress context to save tokens; CLAUDE.md survives compaction. E.g., `/compact focus on auth logic` |\n| `/clear` | Wipe conversation history for a fresh start |\n| `/context` | Visualize context usage as a colored grid with optimization tips |\n| `/cost` | View token usage with per-model and cache-hit breakdowns |\n| `/resume` | Switch to or resume a different session |\n| `/rewind` | Revert to a previous checkpoint in conversation or code |\n| `/btw <question>` | Ask a side question without adding to context cost |\n| `/status` | Show version, connectivity, and session info |\n| `/todos` | List tracked action items from the conversation |\n| `/exit` or `Ctrl+D` | End session |\n\n### Development & Review\n| Command | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `/review` | Request code review of current changes |\n| `/security-review` | Perform security analysis of current changes |\n| `/plan [description]` | Enter Plan mode with auto-start for task planning |\n| `/loop [interval]` | Schedule recurring tasks within the session |\n| `/batch` | Auto-create worktrees for large parallel changes (5-30 worktrees) |\n\n### Configuration & Tools\n| Command | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| `/model [model]` | Switch models mid-session (use arrow keys to adjust effort) |\n| `/effort [level]` | Set reasoning effort: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `max`, or `auto` |\n| `/init` | Create a CLAUDE.md file for project memory |\n| `/memory` | Open CLAUDE.md for editing |\n| `/config` | Open interactive settings configuration |\n| `/permissions` | View/update tool permissions |\n| `/agents` | Manage specialized subagents |\n| `/mcp` | Interactive UI to manage MCP servers |\n| `/add-dir` | Add additional working directories (useful for monorepos) |\n| `/usage` | Show plan limits and rate limit status |\n| `/voice` | Enable push-to-talk voice mode (20 languages; hold Space to record, release to send) |\n| `/release-notes` | Interactive picker for version release notes |\n\n### Custom Slash Commands\nCreate `.claude/commands/<name>.md` (project-shared) or `~/.claude/commands/<name>.md` (personal):\n\n```markdown\n# .claude/commands/deploy.md\nRun the deploy pipeline:\n1. Run all tests\n2. Build the Docker image\n3. Push to registry\n4. Update the $ARGUMENTS environment (default: staging)\n```\n\nUsage: `/deploy production` — `$ARGUMENTS` is replaced with the user's input.\n\n### Skills (Natural Language Invocation)\nUnlike slash commands

{"success": true, "name": "github-repo-management", "description": "Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Repositories", "Git", "Releases", "Secrets", "Configuration"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-pr-workflow", "github-issues"], "content": "---\nname: github-repo-management\ndescription: \"Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Repositories, Git, Releases, Secrets, Configuration]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-pr-workflow, github-issues]\n---\n\n# GitHub Repository Management\n\nCreate, clone, fork, configure, and manage GitHub repositories. Each section shows `gh` first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- **Troubleshooting GitHub CLI Authentication**: If `gh auth status` fails despite having `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported, try:\n  1. Unset the environment variable: `unset GITHUB_TOKEN`\n  2. Run `gh auth login` and authenticate via browser or paste token when prompted\n  3. Alternatively, use direct curl commands with `-H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\"` for API calls\n  4. Note that `gh` commands may not always pick up `GITHUB_TOKEN` from environment in some contexts\n- **Important**: Always verify authentication before proceeding. If `gh auth status` fails, run `gh auth login` to authenticate.\n\n### Setup\n\n```bash\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if [ -f ~/.hermes/.env ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" ~/.hermes/.env; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" ~/.hermes/.env | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\\\([^@]*\\\\)@.*|\\\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\n\n# Get your GitHub username (needed for several operations)\nif [ \"$AUTH\" = \"gh\" ]; then\n  GH_USER=$(gh api user --jq '.login')\nelse\n  GH_USER=$(curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" https://api.github.com/user | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['login'])\")\nfi\n```\n\nIf you're inside a repo already:\n\n```bash\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Cloning Repositories\n\nCloning is pure `git` — works identically either way:\n\n```bash\n# Clone via HTTPS (works with credential helper or token-embedded URL)\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone into a specific directory\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git ./my-local-dir\n\n# Shallow clone (faster for large repos)\ngit clone --depth 1 https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone a specific branch\ngit clone --branch develop https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone via SSH (if SSH is configured)\ngit clone git@github.com:owner/repo-name.git\n```\n\n**With gh (shorthand):**\n\n```bash\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name -- --depth 1\n```\n\n## SOUL.md / Agent Profile Resource Repositories\n\nWhen a Hermes profile `SOUL.md` or agent identity file lists resource repositories to consult or edit, treat it as a repository-management task:\n\n- Read the active profile's `SOUL.md` first and extract bot name, repo URLs, allowed GitHub org/owner boundaries, Git identity, and branch rules.\n- Use a stable profile-local workspace such as `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/resource_repositories/`.\n- Convert GitHub `/tree/<branch>/<path>` URLs to clone URLs, clone/fetch each repo, and check out the bot-name branch (for example `c01entrepreneur_bot`) when present.\n- If `origin/<bot_name>` does not exist, create a local bot-name branch from the remote default branch.\n- Verify origin URL, branch, upstream, HEAD, and dirty status before reporting completion.\n- If a referenced `github.com/<org>/<repo>` returns API `404` or HTTPS clone requests credentials, report it as likely private/inaccessible and ask for GitHub auth/access rather than guessing an alternate source.\n- For the entrepreneur profile/Jordan work, treat the allowed GitHub owner boundary as authoritative: use only `jordatech/*` repositories unless the user explicitly authorizes another owner in the current turn. If the user links a non-`jordatech` repo by mistake and then corrects it, stop using that source, fetch the equivalent `jordatech` source, and verify the remote URL before checkout/copy/commit.\n- In Hermes profile sandboxes, a repo can appear unauthenticated even when `gh auth status` works from the real user home. For `git fetch`, `git pull --rebase`, `git push`, and `git ls-remote`, prefer `HOME=/home/$USER` (and `GH_CONFIG_DIR=/home/$USER/.config/gh` for `gh` if needed). `gh auth setup-git` alone may not fix raw `git` commands when the active profile `HOME` is different from the real user home.\n\nSee `references/soul-resource-repositories.md` for the reusable checkout/sync script pattern, verification checklist, exact-match SOUL sync pitfalls, and pitfalls from the entrepreneur profile setup, including the Hermes profile-HOME vs real-user-HOME GitHub auth quirk.\n\nSee `references/github-auth-troubleshooting.md` for troubleshooting GitHub CLI authentication issues when environment variables are not picked up correctly.\n\nSee `references/noninteractive-fork-import-and-git-auth.md` for the fallback pattern when GitHub forking is disabled, `jordatech` is a user account rather than an org, or HTTPS git pushes fail in non-interactive sessions despite valid `gh` auth.\n\n## 2. Creating Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a public repo and clone it\ngh repo create my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# Private, with description and license\ngh repo create my-new-project --private --description \"A useful tool\" --license MIT --clone\n\n# Under an organization\ngh repo create my-org/my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# From existing local directory\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngh repo create my-project --source . --public --push\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create the remote repo via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/user/repos \\\n  -d '{\n    \"name\": \"my-new-project\",\n    \"description\": \"A useful tool\",\n    \"private\": false,\n    \"auto_init\": true,\n    \"license_template\": \"mit\"\n  }'\n\n# Clone it\ngit clone https://github.com/$GH_USER/my-new-project.git\ncd my-new-project\n\n# -- OR -- push an existing local directory to the new repo\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngit init\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"Initial commit\"\ngit remote add origin https://github.com/$GH_USER/my-new-project.git\ngit push -u origin main\n```\n\nTo create under an organization:\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/orgs/my-org/repos \\\n  -d '{\"name\": \"my-new-project\", \"private\": false}'\n```\n\n### From a Template\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo create my-new-app --template owner/template-repo --public --clone\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/owner/template-repo/generate \\\n  -d '{\"owner\": \"'\"$GH_USER\"'\", \"name\": \"my-new-app\", \"private\": false}'\n```\n\n## 3. Forking Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo fork owner/repo-name --clone\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create the fork via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/owner/repo-name/forks\n\n# Wait a moment for GitHub to create it, then clone\nsleep 3\ngit clone https://github.com/$GH_USER/repo-name.git\ncd repo-name\n\n# Add the original repo as \"upstream\" remote\ngit remote add upstream https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n```\n\n### Keeping a Fork in Sync\n\n```bash\n# Pure git — works everywhere\ngit fetch upstream\ngit checkout main\ngit merge upstream/main\ngit push origin main\n```\n\n**With gh (shortcut):**\n\n```bash\ngh repo sync $GH_USER/repo-name\n```\n\n## 4. Repository Information\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo view owner/repo-name\ngh repo list --limit 20\ngh search repos \"machine learning\" --language python --sort stars\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# View repo details\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nr = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Name: {r['full_name']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Description: {r['description']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Stars: {r['stargazers_count']}  Forks: {r['forks_count']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Default branch: {r['default_branch']}\\\")\nprint(f\\\"Language: {r['language']}\\\")\"\n\n# List your repos\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/user/repos?per_page=20&sort=updated\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    vis = 'private' if r['private'] else 'public'\n    print(f\\\"  {r['full_name']:40}  {vis:8}  {r.get('language', ''):10}  ★{r['stargazers_count']}\\\")\"\n\n# Search repos\ncurl -s \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/search/repositories?q=machine+learning+language:python&sort=stars&per_page=10\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin)['items']:\n    print(f\\\"  {r['full_name']:40}  ★{r['stargazers_count']:6}  {r['description'][:60] if r['description'] else ''}\\\")\"\n```\n\n## 5. Repository Settings\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh repo edit --description \"Updated description\" --visibility public\ngh repo edit --enable-wiki=false --enable-issues=true\ngh repo edit --default-branch main\ngh repo edit --add-topic \"machine-learning,python\"\ngh repo edit --enable-auto-merge\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\ncurl -s -X PATCH \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO \\\n  -d '{\n    \"description\": \"Updated description\",\n    \"has_wiki\": false,\n    \"has_issues\": true,\n    \"allow_auto_merge\": true\n  }'\n\n# Update topics\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.mercy-preview+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/topics \\\n  -d '{\"names\": [\"machine-learning\", \"python\", \"automation\"]}'\n```\n\n## 6. Branch Protection\n\n```bash\n# View current protection\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/branches/main/protection\n\n# Set up branch protection\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/branches/main/protection \\\n  -d '{\n    \"required_status_checks\": {\n      \"strict\": true,\n      \"contexts\": [\"ci/test\", \"ci/lint\"]\n    },\n    \"enforce_admins\": false,\n    \"required_pull_request_reviews\": {\n      \"required_approving_review_count\": 1\n    },\n    \"restrictions\": null\n  }'\n```\n\n## 7. Secrets Management (GitHub Actions)\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh secret set API_KEY --body \"your-secret-value\"\ngh secret set SSH_KEY < ~/.ssh/id_rsa\ngh secret list\ngh secret delete API_KEY\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\nSecrets require encryption with the repo's public key — more involved via API:\n\n```bash\n# Get the repo's public key for encrypting secrets\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets/public-key\n\n# Encrypt and set (requires Python with PyNaCl)\npython3 -c \"\nfrom base64 import b64encode\nfrom nacl import encoding, public\nimport json, sys\n\n# Get the public key\nkey_id = '<key_id_from_above>'\npublic_key = '<base64_key_from_above>'\n\n# Encrypt\nsealed = public.SealedBox(\n    public.PublicKey(public_key.encode('utf-8'), encoding.Base64Encoder)\n).encrypt('your-secret-value'.encode('utf-8'))\nprint(json.dumps({\n    'encrypted_value': b64encode(sealed).decode('utf-8'),\n    'key_id': key_id\n}))\"\n\n# Then PUT the encrypted secret\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets/API_KEY \\\n  -d '<output from python script above>'\n\n# List secrets (names only, values hidden)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/secrets \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor s in json.load(sys.stdin)['secrets']:\n    print(f\\\"  {s['name']:30}  updated: {s['updated_at']}\\\")\"\n```\n\nNote: For secrets, `gh secret set` is dramatically simpler. If setting secrets is needed and `gh` isn't available, recommend installing it for just that operation.\n\n## 8. Releases\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh release create v1.0.0 --title \"v1.0.0\" --generate-notes\ngh release create v2.0.0-rc1 --draft --prerelease --generate-notes\ngh release create v1.0.0 ./dist/binary --title \"v1.0.0\" --notes \"Release notes\"\ngh release list\ngh release download v1.0.0 --dir ./downloads\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a release\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases \\\n  -d '{\n    \"tag_name\": \"v1.0.0\",\n    \"name\": \"v1.0.0\",\n    \"body\": \"## Changelog\\n- Feature A\\n- Bug fix B\",\n    \"draft\": false,\n    \"prerelease\": false,\n    \"generate_release_notes\": true\n  }'\n\n# List releases\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    tag = r.get('tag_name', 'no tag')\n    print(f\\\"  {tag:15}  {r['name']:30}  {'draft' if r['draft'] else 'published'}\\\")\"\n\n# Upload a release asset (binary file)\nRELEASE_ID=<id_from_create_response>\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/octet-stream\" \\\n  \"https://uploads.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/releases/$RELEASE_ID/assets?name=binary-amd64\" \\\n  --data-binary @./dist/binary-amd64\n```\n\n## 9. GitHub Actions Workflows\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh workflow list\ngh run list --limit 10\ngh run view <RUN_ID>\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\ngh run rerun <RUN_ID>\ngh run rerun <RUN_ID> --failed\ngh workflow run ci.yml --ref main\ngh workflow run deploy.yml -f environment=staging\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# List workflows\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/workflows \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor w in json.load(sys.stdin)['workflows']:\n    print(f\\\"  {w['id']:10}  {w['name']:30}  {w['state']}\\\")\"\n\n# List recent runs\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?per_page=10\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor r in json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']:\n    print(f\\\"  Run {r['id']}  {r['name']:30}  {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Download failed run logs\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs\n\n# Re-run a failed workflow\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/rerun\n\n# Re-run only failed jobs\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/rerun-failed-jobs\n\n# Trigger a workflow manually (workflow_dispatch)\nWORKFLOW_ID=<workflow_id_or_filename>\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/workflows/$WORKFLOW_ID/dispatches \\\n  -d '{\"ref\": \"main\", \"inputs\": {\"environment\": \"staging\"}}'\n```\n\n## 10. Gists\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh gist create script.py --public --desc \"Useful script\"\ngh gist list\n```\n\n**With curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Create a gist\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/gists \\\n  -d '{\n    \"description\": \"Useful script\",\n    \"public\": true,\n    \"files\": {\n      \"script.py\": {\"content\": \"print(\\\"hello\\\")\"}\n    }\n  }'\n\n# List your gists\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/gists \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor g in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    files = ', '.join(g['files'].keys())\n    print(f\\\"  {g['id']}  {g['description'] or '(no desc)':40}  {files}\\\")\"\n```\n\n## Quick Reference Table\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| Clone | `gh repo clone o/r` | `git clone https://github.com/o/r.git` |\n| Create repo | `gh repo create name --public` | `curl POST /user/repos` |\n| Fork | `gh repo fork o/r --clone` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/forks` + `git clone` |\n| Repo info | `gh repo view o/r` | `curl GET /repos/o/r` |\n| Edit settings | `gh repo edit --...` | `curl PATCH /repos/o/r` |\n| Create release | `gh release create v1.0` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/releases` |\n| List workflows | `gh workflow list` | `curl GET /repos/o/r/actions/workflows` |\n| Rerun CI | `gh run rerun ID` | `curl POST /repos/o/r/actions/runs/ID/rerun` |\n| Set secret | `gh secret set KEY` | `curl PUT /repos/o/r/actions/secrets/KEY` (+ encryption) |\n", "path": {"success": true, "name": "business-idea-systems", "description": "Build and operate business-idea repositories, validation workflows, and lightweight idea-viewer apps.", "tags": ["entrepreneurship", "business-ideas", "market-research", "customer-discovery", "SaaS", "automation"], "related_skills": ["github-repo-management", "github-pr-workflow", "hermes-agent", "popular-web-designs"], "content": "---\nname: business-idea-systems\ndescription: \"Build and operate business-idea repositories, validation workflows, and lightweight idea-viewer apps.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: c01entrepreneur_bot\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [entrepreneurship, business-ideas, market-research, customer-discovery, SaaS, automation]\n    related_skills: [github-repo-management, github-pr-workflow, hermes-agent, popular-web-designs]\n---\n\n# Business Idea Systems\n\nUse this skill when the user asks to generate, store, display, validate, or automate business ideas, SaaS niches, customer personas, market research plans, or entrepreneurship knowledge systems.\n\nThe goal is not just to brainstorm ideas. The goal is to produce a durable system that software teams, sales teams, and founders can use: human-readable ideas, structured metadata, assumptions to validate, and a repeatable customer-discovery loop.\n\n## Triggers\n\nUse this skill when asked to:\n\n- Create a business idea generator, niche hunter, opportunity database, or SaaS idea repository.\n- Convert entrepreneurship frameworks into reusable templates, prompts, or product screens.\n- Generate a business idea around a persona, pain, market, or product category.\n- Develop a partial business-model brief into a full market, MVBP, revenue, risk, and validation plan.\n- Store ideas in GitHub, Obsidian, markdown, JSON, Airtable, Notion, or a web app.\n- Set up recurring idea generation or recurring improvements to the idea-generation system.\n- Add or maintain recurring opportunity-source locations such as Reddit communities, forums, directories, LinkedIn Jobs signals, or trend feeds.\n- Build a private founder dashboard or Vercel/Next.js viewer for idea content.\n\n## Core Principle\n\nStart with **persona and pain**, not product features.\n\nA strong idea entry should answer:\n\n1. Who is the specific persona?\n2. What painful situation do they repeatedly experience?\n3. What outcome are they already trying to achieve?\n4. What alternatives or workarounds do they use today?\n5. What assumptions must be validated before building?\n6. What is the minimum viable business product / MVBP?\n7. What market research conversation should happen next?\n\n## Recommended Source Frameworks\n\nBlend these frameworks when creating or evaluating an idea:\n\n- **Bill Aulet / Disciplined Entrepreneurship:** beachhead market, end user profile, TAM, persona, full lifecycle use case, quantified value proposition, MVBP, pricing, sales process, key assumptions.\n- **Paul Cheek / Startup Tactics:** tactical implementation of Disciplined Entrepreneurship and structured experimentation.\n- **Allan Dib / One Page Marketing Plan:** before/during/after marketing, target market, message, media, lead capture, nurturing, conversion, lifetime value, referrals.\n- **Giff Constable / Talking to Humans + Testing with Humans:** assumptions mapping, customer discovery, lightweight experiments, evidence quality.\n- **Rob Fitzpatrick / The Mom Test:** ask about past behavior and specifics; avoid compliments, hypotheticals, and pitching.\n\nWhen Jordan asks for a critical market-validation audit across a portfolio, roleplay the advisor panel but make the output operational: add idea-specific validation status, priority, recruiting channel, evidence required, experiments, data to capture, and kill/advance criteria. Move concrete validation steps up into the `Test the riskiest assumption first` area or equivalent early validation section so the founder sees the next test before reading long critique. Never leave advisor-panel commentary generic across every idea: each Bill Aulet / Paul Cheek / Allan Dib / Giff Constable / Rob Fitzpatrick / skeptical investor comment should mention the idea's actual title, persona, pain, market/category, MRR ambition, or execution risk. See `references/expert-panel-market-validation-model.md`.\n\n## Idea Document Shape\n\nPrefer human-readable Markdown with YAML frontmatter when the user wants durable Git-backed ideas.\n\nMinimum frontmatter:\n\n```yaml\ntitle: \"Idea title\"\nslug: \"idea-title\"\ndate: \"YYYY-MM-DD\"\npersona: \"specific user segment\"\npain: \"specific painful situation\"\ncategory: \"SaaS / technology / market category\"\ntags:\n  - SaaS\n  - customer discovery\nstatus: draft\nconfidence: medium-low until validated\ncreated_by: c01entrepreneur_bot\n```\n\nRecommended body sections:\n\n```markdown\n# Idea Title\n\n## One-line summary\n\n## Next actions\n\n## Persona\n\n## Pain\n\n## Product Concept\n\n## Business Model\n\n## Assumptions to Validate\n\n## First 10 clients\n\nFor every new business idea, include at least 10 specific prospective clients, communities, partners, or buyer organizations. Each entry must include a linked client name, the client-specific need tied to the idea, and a public contact path such as a contact page, demo form, partnership page, community, or support route. Treat this as outbound/customer-discovery source intelligence, not validation.\n\n## Preliminary Market Research Strategy\n\n## Mom Test Interview Questions\n\n## Minimum Viable Business Product\n\n## Risks\n```\n\nPut `Next actions` directly under the one-line summary for founder-facing briefs. The user should see what to do next before reading the long model. Add a `First 10 clients` section near the top of every idea so the founder immediately sees who to interview or sell to first; include at least 10 specific linked prospects with their likely need and a public contact path. Treat these as outbound/customer-discovery hypotheses, not validated customers. Validate portfolio-wide edits with `scripts/validate_first_10_clients.py`; see `references/first-10-clients-prospect-lists.md`. When reordering existing idea Markdown, also update any local viewer snapshots (`data/ideas/*.md`) so deployed fallback content stays consistent.\n\nFor Niche Hunter-style opportunity views, avoid arbitrary scores. Store and calculate `goodness_score` from explicit inputs: Competition, Potential, Est. MRR, and Difficulty. Include a visible formula/rationale plus opportunity analysis, market gap, recommended move, market analysis snapshot, linked potential competitors, key learnings, improvement opportunities, and risks. See `references/opportunity-goodness-scoring.md` and `references/competitor-analysis-pattern.md`.\n\nWhen Jordan provides a batch of rough ideas, domains, personas, pains, prototype links, or monetization notes, convert them into one durable brief per idea instead of only summarizing in chat. Preserve the source intent, normalize each concept into a clear slug/title/persona/pain/product, add Niche Hunter-style scoring and competitor analysis, update `data/ideas.json`, sync the viewer snapshot, build, commit/push, deploy, and verify Basic Auth. See `references/batch-idea-intake-and-analysis.md`.\n\nWhen Jordan provides one rough idea/domain plus an external article, use the article as source intelligence for pricing, risks, COGS, and positioning — not as buyer validation. If `bs4` is unavailable during article extraction, use a stdlib `html.parser` fallback rather than adding a dependency for a one-off scrape. See `references/single-idea-source-article-intake.md`.\n\nWhen adding or changing an idea in `business_idea_generator`, treat `data/` as a checked-in fallback snapshot that the app uses when GitHub API is unavailable. Copy the updated `ideas.json`, copy the changed idea Markdown, check for any indexed Markdown missing from `data/ideas/`, validate JSON, run the viewer build, commit, and push. See `references/business-ideas-source-to-viewer-sync.md`.\n\nWhen converting business ideas into requirements and test cases, use the `jordatech/requirements_management_obsidian` template pattern: keep Obsidian wikilinks intact, avoid `.obsidian/` edits unless requested, preserve fixed filenames when instructed, and generate concise system/software requirement plus system/software test-case examples from each idea's persona and pain. When the user wants those examples added into both `business_ideas` and `business_idea_generator`, copy the checked-in `BUSINESS_IDEA_REQUIREMENT_EXAMPLES.md` into the source repo root and the viewer repo `data/` snapshot, add a founder-visible `/requirements-examples` page in the Next.js app plus nav links, then rebuild, deploy, and verify the protected Vercel alias still returns `401` without credentials. See `references/business-ideas-to-obsidian-requirements.md`.\n\nWhen Jordan asks to create or update Disciplined Entrepreneurship Canvas / One-Page Marketing Plan artifacts from venture briefs, framework dependencies, templates, UX audits, or advisor roleplay, produce durable `.xlsx` / `.docx` / `.md` files rather than only chat analysis. Use Bill Aulet / Allan Dib / Giff Constable / Paul Cheek as distinct roundtable personas when requested; run the requested staged debate, preserve source traceability, add evidence-map sheets for UX audits, and verify generated Office files by reopening them. For Google Drive UX audit folders, recurse through subfolders and resolve Drive shortcuts before exporting Docs/Sheets/PDFs. See `references/de-canvas-opmp-advisor-roundtable.md`.\n\n## Live Findings from the 2026-10-03 Daily-Idea Run (AfterHours AI, idea #94)\n\n- **DeepAPI is the working web-research path** (env `~/.deepapi/env`, key `DEEPAPI_API_KEY`):\n  `POST $DEEPAPI_API_BASE_URL/v1/search/web` REQUIRES a unique `Idempotency-Key` header per\n  call and uses `query`/`maxResults` (NOT `prompt`) in the body; results live at\n  `output[].results[]` with `title`+`url`. Scraping = `POST /v1/scrape/website` with\n  `{url}` → `output[].text`. Run 3–5 differently-worded searches for pricing/competitor/\n  demand grounding before writing a brief; put the real URLs in `source_urls`.\n- **ideas.json `file` field convention: `ideas/<slug>.md` (relative to `data/`)** — writing\n  `data/ideas/<slug>.md` doubles the path and `npm run build` fails on the dynamic\n  `[slug]` page (ENOENT `data/data/ideas/...`). JSON validation does NOT catch this; only\n  the build does. Always rebuild (`npm run build`) after index changes.\n- **The protected viewer's Vercel project is NOT git-connected** (verified 2026-10-03:\n  last prod deploy was 89 days old while main had newer commits). Pushes do NOT\n  auto-deploy. Manual prod deploy from the repo checkout:\n  `vercel deploy --prod --yes --token <vcp_...>` (~40s, auto-aliased to\n  businessideagenerator-three.vercel.app). Reconnect Git integration to restore\n  push-to-deploy. The VERCEL_TOKEN in `~/.bashrc` is QUOTED — `tr -d '\"'` before\n  passing to `--token` (the CLI errors on embedded quotes).\n- **Basic Auth creds are NOT retrievable via `vercel env pull`** — sensitive values come\n  back as empty strings. The working `jordan:<pass>` pair is recovered from prior session\n  archives (session_search) and must be staged to a 0600 file, never echoed or committed.\n- **ideas.json is build-time input, NOT a served route** — `/data/ideas.json` 404s on prod\n  (good: private data is not publicly fetchable). Verify deployed content by curling the\n  idea page authed and grepping for distinctive phrases from the repo markdown.\n- **First-10-clients validator entry format (from the validator regex):** each of the 10\n  entries must be a NUMBERED list item with the link FIRST — `1. **[Name](url)** —\n  **Specific need:** ... **How to contact:** ...` — bold-link at the start of the line,\n  then the literal phrases `Specific need:` and `How to contact:`. Unnumbered or\n  link-after-prose entries fail the validator (sections/entries/needs/contacts all\n  counted separately). Most pre-existing ideas score 0 on it — that's their pre-existing\n  state; don't \"fix\" the whole portfolio in an idea-adding run.\n- **Git direction (Jordan-directed 2026-10-03):** `startupteams/business_idea_generator`\n  (org, source) → `jordatech/business_idea_generator` (personal, deploy mirror). A one-way\n  sync workflow now exists in the mirror (`.github/workflows/sync-from-org-source.yml`):\n  dispatch/poll triggers, already-synced no-op via merge-base check, FF when aligned,\n  sync-branch+PR when diverged, `synced/<sha>` marker tags, no reverse sync. Daily ideas\n  land on the mirror; the first mirror→org convergence was a one-time FF (flagged to Jordan).\n- **Pre-existing branch-name collision trap:** `git checkout -b AGENT_STEA004_ENTREPRENEUR`\n  fails silently-noisy when the branch already exists and the commit lands on main. Run\n  `git branch --show-current` BEFORE committing (this bit the 10-03 run; the pre-push\n  guard caught the main push and required the audited override).\n\n## Idea Data Access Pattern\n\nWhen you need to read idea data (idea lists, idea content, frontmatter) from the business_idea_generator, **prefer the local repo at `/home/jordatech/business_idea_generator/`** over scraping the deployed Vercel app. The local repo has:\n- `data/ideas.json` — structured index with all frontmatter fields\n- `data/ideas/*.md` — 83+ individual idea files with full business models\n- `docs/` — improvement backlog, sprint planning, source intelligence docs\n\nOnly use `curl -u \"user:pass\" \"https://businessideagenerator-three.vercel.app/...\"` for the deployed app's rendered HTML (e.g., verifying a deployed page works). The Vercel app is a Next.js SPA — its HTML is rendered client-side and `grep` for idea slugs will return incomplete results. The local repo is the source of truth.\n\nWhen the user says \"the businessideagenerator is here on GitHub\", they mean `github.com/jordatech/business_idea_generator` and the local clone at `/home/jordatech/business_idea_generator/`.\n\n## Repository Pattern\n\nAs of July 2026, `business_idea_generator` is the **single repository**. The old `business_ideas` repo is deprecated and archived. All ideas, indexes, viewer code, and fallback data live in `business_idea_generator` only.\n\nFor a private GitHub-backed idea system:\n\n1. Store ideas under `data/ideas/<slug>.md` (checked-in fallback snapshot).\n2. Maintain a lightweight index at `data/ideas.json`.\n3. Keep secrets out of the repository. Use environment variables for private repo access.\n4. Use a bot branch for implementation work when the active agent profile defines one.\n5. Commit meaningful checkpoints, but do not commit more than needed.\n6. The app tries GitHub first (if `GITHUB_TOKEN` is set), then falls back to local `data/` files.\n\n### Private GitHub + Vercel Pattern\n\nThe viewer app reads from a single repo: `jordatech/business_idea_generator`. The old `business_ideas` repo is archived and should not be used.\n\n- Add `.env.example` documenting required variables only.\n- Use `GITHUB_TOKEN` at runtime/build time when live private-repo refresh is required.\n- Store the real token in Vercel environment variables or GitHub Actions secrets, never in source.\n- For a deployable viewer that should work **without secrets**, check in a sanitized local snapshot such as `data/ideas.json` plus `data/ideas/*.md`, and make the app try GitHub first, then fall back to local files.\n- Document `GITHUB_TOKEN` as optional when a checked-in snapshot exists; the token becomes an enhancement for live refresh, not a hard deploy dependency.\n- If Vercel CLI is unauthenticated, authenticate via API key environment variable: `export VERCEL_TOKEN=\"vcp_...\"`, write to `~/.bashrc` for persistence. **Do NOT use `vercel login --token X`** — the CLI rejects this flag. Device code flow (`vercel login`) also fails through browser automation due to OAuth redirect loops to blank pages. Google OAuth with passkey-only accounts (no password) cannot be automated; always prefer the env var approach. **When invoking `vercel deploy`, pass the token as a literal string** (`--token \"vcp_...\"`) rather than relying on shell variable expansion (`--token \"$VERCEL_TOKEN\"`), which can fail silently with \"missing a value\" errors if the shell environment doesn't source `~/.bashrc` in the current session.\n- If Vercel CLI is not installed and `npm install -g vercel` fails with EACCES (permission denied), install under user's home: `npm install -g --prefix \"$HOME/.local\" vercel` and run as `\"$HOME/.local/bin/vercel\"`.\n- If `.vercel/` directory has stale project linkage causing \"Could not retrieve Project Settings\" errors, remove `.vercel/` and redeploy with `vercel deploy --prod --yes`.\n- If Vercel token expires, CLI commands return \"You are not authorized\" or \"token is not valid\". The token must be refreshed from the Vercel dashboard → Settings → Tokens. There is no CLI-side refresh path.\n- For Basic Auth-protected production deployments, a browser auth failure or unauthenticated `401` can be expected. Verify safely with `curl -I` and `curl -s -u user:pass` rather than exposing credentials. See `references/focused-idea-query-view-and-vercel-basic-auth-verification.md`.\n\n## Portfolio Reorganization (Prune / Merge / Curate)\n\nWhen the idea portfolio grows beyond ~50 entries, it needs periodic reorganization: prune weak ideas, merge overlapping ones, and curate survivors into actionable tiers. This is also the natural follow-up when a Fable 5 strategic analysis produces instructions to reorganize the portfolio.\n\n### Procedure\n\n1. **Ingest:** Read all idea files from `data/ideas/*.md`, parse YAML frontmatter, build a table (slug, title, category, persona, pain, goodness_score, revenue_model_present, body_word_count). Write to `data/reorg/00_inventory.md`.\n2. **Prune (mechanical kill rules):**\n   - P1: `goodness_score < 65`\n   - P2: No revenue model in body (no pricing, no buyer who pays)\n   - P3: Persona undefined or \"everyone/consumers\" with no segment\n   - P4: Requires regulatory license AND score < 75\n   - P5: Body < 200 words (never developed enough)\n   - Exception: if an idea fails P1-P5 but is the highest-scoring merge candidate in its category, mark SALVAGE-INTO-MERGE instead of KILL.\n   - Write killed list to `data/reorg/01_pruned.md`.\n3. **Web-validate survivors:** For each surviving idea, search for competitors and funding signals. Classify as CROWDED (4+ funded competitors), VALIDATED (1-3 competitors, evidence of demand), or EMPTY (0 competitors — flag for human review). Write to `data/reorg/02_validation.md`.\n4. **Merge overlapping ideas:** Two ideas are merge candidates if they share persona (M1), pain (M2), technology where one is a feature of the other (M3), or same category with 2+ survivors and overlapping product surfaces (M4). The higher-scoring idea survives; others are absorbed. Never merge more than 4 into one. Write merge log to `data/reorg/03_merges.md`.\n5. **Curate into 3 tiers:**\n   - **Tier 1 — Standalone bets:** Distinct buyer, distinct go-to-market, moat that dies as a feature (marketplaces, infrastructure, insurance, manufacturing, compute). Rank by goodness_score, then market verdict.\n   - **Tier 2 — Fold into AgentifyMe:** Buyer would plausibly already be an AgentifyMe customer, idea is a workflow AI agents could deliver, no separate regulatory/hardware/marketplace dependency.\n   - **Tier 3 — Parked:** Score ≥ 75 but market verdict is EMPTY or UNKNOWN, or depends on a capability that doesn't exist yet.\n6. **Produce output:** Write `data/reorg/FINAL.md` (Tier 1/2/3 + Killed appendix + Flagged for human review) and `data/reorg/final_ideas.json` (array with slug, title, tier, score, category, merged_from, market_verdict).\n7. **Accounting check (CRITICAL):** Every input slug must appear exactly once across the output — as
