{"success": true, "name": "github-pr-workflow", "description": "GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"], "content": "---\nname: github-pr-workflow\ndescription: \"GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Pull-Requests, CI/CD, Git, Automation, Merge]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-code-review]\n---\n\n# GitHub Pull Request Workflow\n\nComplete guide for managing the PR lifecycle. Each section shows the `gh` way first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback for machines without `gh`.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- Inside a git repository with a GitHub remote\n\n### Quick Auth Detection\n\n```bash\n# Determine which method to use throughout this workflow\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  # Ensure we have a token for API calls\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if _hermes_env=\"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/.env\"; [ -f \"$_hermes_env\" ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\"; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\" | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\necho \"Using: $AUTH\"\n```\n\n### Extracting Owner/Repo from the Git Remote\n\nMany `curl` commands need `owner/repo`. Extract it from the git remote:\n\n```bash\n# Works for both HTTPS and SSH remote URLs\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\necho \"Owner: $OWNER, Repo: $REPO\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Branch Creation\n\nThis part is pure `git` — identical either way:\n\n```bash\n# Make sure you're up to date\ngit fetch origin\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# Create and switch to a new branch\ngit checkout -b feat/add-user-authentication\n```\n\nBranch naming conventions:\n- `feat/description` — new features\n- `fix/description` — bug fixes\n- `refactor/description` — code restructuring\n- `docs/description` — documentation\n- `ci/description` — CI/CD changes\n\n## 2. Making Commits\n\nUse the agent's file tools (`write_file`, `patch`) to make changes, then commit:\n\n```bash\n# Stage specific files\ngit add src/auth.py src/models/user.py tests/test_auth.py\n\n# Commit with a conventional commit message\ngit commit -m \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\n\n- Add login/register endpoints\n- Add User model with password hashing\n- Add auth middleware for protected routes\n- Add unit tests for auth flow\"\n```\n\nCommit message format (Conventional Commits):\n```\ntype(scope): short description\n\nLonger explanation if needed. Wrap at 72 characters.\n```\n\nTypes: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ci`, `chore`, `perf`\n\n## 3. Pushing and Creating a PR\n\n### Push the Branch (same either way)\n\n```bash\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n```\n\n### Create the PR\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --title \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\" \\\n  --body \"## Summary\n- Adds login and register API endpoints\n- JWT token generation and validation\n\n## Test Plan\n- [ ] Unit tests pass\n\nCloses #42\"\n```\n\nOptions: `--draft`, `--reviewer user1,user2`, `--label \"enhancement\"`, `--base develop`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"title\\\": \\\"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\\\",\n    \\\"body\\\": \\\"## Summary\\nAdds login and register API endpoints.\\n\\nCloses #42\\\",\n    \\\"head\\\": \\\"$BRANCH\\\",\n    \\\"base\\\": \\\"main\\\"\n  }\"\n```\n\nThe response JSON includes the PR `number` — save it for later commands.\n\nTo create as a draft, add `\"draft\": true` to the JSON body.\n\n## 4. Monitoring CI Status\n\n### Check CI Status\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# One-shot check\ngh pr checks\n\n# Watch until all checks finish (polls every 10s)\ngh pr checks --watch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Get the latest commit SHA on the current branch\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n# Query the combined status\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Overall: {data['state']}\\\")\nfor s in data.get('statuses', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {s['context']}: {s['state']} - {s.get('description', '')}\\\")\"\n\n# Also check GitHub Actions check runs (separate endpoint)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/check-runs \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nfor cr in data.get('check_runs', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {cr['name']}: {cr['status']} / {cr['conclusion'] or 'pending'}\\\")\"\n```\n\n### Poll Until Complete (git + curl)\n\n```bash\n# Simple polling loop — check every 30 seconds, up to 10 minutes\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s \\\n    -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n    https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n    | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\")\n  echo \"Check $i: $STATUS\"\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" = \"success\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"failure\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"error\" ]; then\n    break\n  fi\n  sleep 30\ndone\n```\n\n## 5. Auto-Fixing CI Failures\n\nWhen CI fails, diagnose and fix. This loop works with either auth method.\n\n### Step 1: Get Failure Details\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# List recent workflow runs on this branch\ngh run list --branch $(git branch --show-current) --limit 5\n\n# View failed logs\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\n# List workflow runs on this branch\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?branch=$BRANCH&per_page=5\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nruns = json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']\nfor r in runs:\n    print(f\\\"Run {r['id']}: {r['name']} - {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Get failed job logs (download as zip, extract, read)\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs && cat ci-logs/*.txt\n```\n\n### Step 2: Fix and Push\n\nAfter identifying the issue, use file tools (`patch`, `write_file`) to fix it:\n\n```bash\ngit add <fixed_files>\ngit commit -m \"fix: resolve CI failure in <check_name>\"\ngit push\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify\n\nRe-check CI status using the commands from Section 4 above.\n\n### Auto-Fix Loop Pattern\n\nWhen asked to auto-fix CI, follow this loop:\n\n1. Check CI status → identify failures\n2. Read failure logs → understand the error\n3. Use `read_file` + `patch`/`write_file` → fix the code\n4. `git add . && git commit -m \"fix: ...\" && git push`\n5. Wait for CI → re-check status\n6. Repeat if still failing (up to 3 attempts, then ask the user)\n\n## 6. Merging\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Squash merge + delete branch (cleanest for feature branches)\ngh pr merge --squash --delete-branch\n\n# Enable auto-merge (merges when all checks pass)\ngh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nPR_NUMBER=<number>\n\n# Merge the PR via API (squash)\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER/merge \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"merge_method\\\": \\\"squash\\\",\n    \\\"commit_title\\\": \\\"feat: add user authentication (#$PR_NUMBER)\\\"\n  }\"\n\n# Delete the remote branch after merge\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\ngit push origin --delete $BRANCH\n\n# Switch back to main locally\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\ngit branch -d $BRANCH\n```\n\nMerge methods: `\"merge\"` (merge commit), `\"squash\"`, `\"rebase\"`\n\n### Enable Auto-Merge (curl)\n\n```bash\n# Auto-merge requires the repo to have it enabled in settings.\n# This uses the GraphQL API since REST doesn't support auto-merge.\nPR_NODE_ID=$(curl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER \\\n  | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['node_id'])\")\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/graphql \\\n  -d \"{\\\"query\\\": \\\"mutation { enablePullRequestAutoMerge(input: {pullRequestId: \\\\\\\"$PR_NODE_ID\\\\\\\", mergeMethod: SQUASH}) { clientMutationId } }\\\"}\"\n```\n\n## 7. Complete Workflow Example\n\n```bash\n# 1. Start from clean main\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# 2. Branch\ngit checkout -b fix/login-redirect-bug\n\n# 3. (Agent makes code changes with file tools)\n\n# 4. Commit\ngit add src/auth/login.py tests/test_login.py\ngit commit -m \"fix: correct redirect URL after login\n\nPreserves the ?next= parameter instead of always redirecting to /dashboard.\"\n\n# 5. Push\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n\n# 6. Create PR (picks gh or curl based on what's available)\n# ... (see Section 3)\n\n# 7. Monitor CI (see Section 4)\n\n# 8. Merge when green (see Section 6)\n```\n\n## Useful PR Commands Reference\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| List my PRs | `gh pr list --author @me` | `curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls?state=open\"` |\n| View PR diff | `gh pr diff` | `git diff main...HEAD` (local) or `curl -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.diff\" ...` |\n| Add comment | `gh pr comment N --body \"...\"` | `curl -X POST .../issues/N/comments -d '{\"body\":\"...\"}'` |\n| Request review | `gh pr edit N --add-reviewer user` | `curl -X POST .../pulls/N/requested_reviewers -d '{\"reviewers\":[\"user\"]}'` |\n| Close PR | `gh pr close N` | `curl -X PATCH .../pulls/N -d '{\"state\":\"closed\"}'` |\n| Check out someone's PR | `gh pr checkout N` | `git fetch origin pull/N/head:pr-N && git checkout pr-N` |\n", "path": "github/github-pr-workflow/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/github/github-pr-workflow", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/ci-troubleshooting.md", "references/conventional-commits.md"], "templates": ["templates/pr-body-bugfix.md", "templates/pr-body-feature.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "hermes-file-delivery", "description": "Deliver files to the user through Hermes messaging platforms (Telegram etc.). THE mechanism is the MEDIA:<path> tag in your final reply — there is no send tool to search for. FIRST skill to load for any 'send/upload/put this file on Telegram' request. Covers the tag, MEDIA_DELIVERY_EXTS allowlist (.md works), [[as_document]] directive, adapter send paths, troubleshooting.", "tags": ["hermes", "telegram", "file-delivery", "gateway", "handoff"], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: hermes-file-delivery\ndescription: \"Deliver files to the user through Hermes messaging platforms (Telegram etc.). THE mechanism is the MEDIA:<path> tag in your final reply — there is no send tool to search for. FIRST skill to load for any 'send/upload/put this file on Telegram' request. Covers the tag, MEDIA_DELIVERY_EXTS allowlist (.md works), [[as_document]] directive, adapter send paths, troubleshooting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, telegram, file-delivery, gateway, handoff]\n---\n\n# Hermes File Delivery (messaging platforms)\n\nClass of task: the user asks for a file (handoff .md, report, artifact) to be\n\"uploaded to Telegram so I can download it on my phone\". The mechanism is NOT a\nsend tool — it is a **tag in your final reply** that the gateway parses after\nthe text is delivered.\n\n## The mechanism (verified in gateway source, 2026-09-26)\n\n1. Put a literal tag line in your FINAL assistant response:\n\n       MEDIA:/absolute/path/to/file.md\n\n   - Absolute path, real file, extension from the allowlist below.\n   - The gateway's `_deliver_media_from_response` (gateway/run.py) extracts\n     tags via `adapter.extract_media` and sends each file through the platform\n     adapter (`send_document` for doc-like files; images go sendPhoto unless\n     `[[as_document]]` is present anywhere in the reply).\n   - Keep the tag on its own line; optional one-line lead-in text is fine\n     (text streams first, then the attachment arrives).\n\n2. Extension allowlist = `MEDIA_DELIVERY_EXTS` in\n   `gateway/platforms/base.py` (~line 1401). Includes **.md**, .txt, .pdf,\n   .csv, .json, .yaml, .zip, images/audio/video, .html. If your extension is\n   missing, copy/transcode to a listed one (e.g. `.md` → fine as-is; unknown\n   binary → zip it).\n\n3. Directives you may combine in the same reply:\n   - `[[as_document]]` — deliver image-extension files as lossless documents\n     (sendDocument) instead of recompressed photos. All-or-nothing per reply.\n   - `[[audio_as_voice]]` — TTS/audio delivered as a voice bubble.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- **\"Send this over Telegram\" ≠ find a send tool — READ THIS SKILL FIRST.**\n  This failure has recurred TWICE (2026-09-28 and again 2026-09-29: multiple\n  tool_search round-trips hunting a send tool, two attempts at an unloaded\n  `send_message` tool, then a raw Bot API script — while the one-step answer\n  was sitting here). When a file-delivery request arrives, the DEFAULT is:\n  write/verify the file, then put `MEDIA:<abs-path>` on its own line in the\n  final reply. Do not tool_search for send mechanisms. There is a\n  `send_message` tool in the codebase (tools/send_message_tool.py, supports\n  `MEDIA:<path>` in message text) but it is NOT wired into this profile's\n  runtime toolset — do not go looking; the tag path needs nothing.\n  **Session-start trigger:** if the request says \"send/attach/deliver … over\n  Telegram (as a document/file)\", load THIS skill before making any tool\n  calls about delivery.\n\n- **Last-resort fallback (gateway path unavailable):** Bot API sendDocument\n  works from execute_code/terminal — read the active `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` from\n  the profile `.env` (uncommented line; never print it), take the chat_id from\n  `TELEGRAM_HOME_CHANNEL` (same file) or `channel_directory.json`, POST\n  multipart `sendDocument` with caption. Verify `ok: true` + `message_id` in\n  the response. Proven 2026-09-29 (16 KB .md handoff → home chat, doc\n  attachment + caption). Note the deliverable then lands OUTSIDE the gateway's\n  session history — prefer the tag whenever the gateway is up.\n- **Prose mentions don't deliver.** The tag must appear as a real `MEDIA:`\n  token in the final reply — writing \"the file is at /path/file.md\" does\n  nothing. Conversely, never write `MEDIA:` with a fake path in prose\n  (docs, examples) — masked spans (code blocks, JSON strings) are protected,\n  but bare ones in plain text WILL fire a delivery of a nonexistent path.\n- **Auto-append only fires for producer tools** (`text_to_speech`,\n  `image_generate`) whose tool RESULTS contain MEDIA: lines. A MEDIA: line in\n  a generic tool result or a non-producer tool's output is NOT auto-appended —\n  emit the tag yourself in the final reply.\n- **Files must exist at send time** on the gateway host; the adapter fails the\n  attachment silently (logged warning) if the path is missing — verify the\n  file was actually written (skill: file-write-verification) before tagging.\n- Large files: Telegram bots cap documents at 50 MB via the standard API.\n- The tag is STRIPPED from user-visible text — don't also describe it as a\n  link in the same line.\n\n## Where this lives in source (for future re-verification)\n\n- `gateway/run.py` — `_deliver_media_from_response`, `_TOOL_MEDIA_RE`\n  (tool-result auto-append matcher), `_AUTO_APPEND_MEDIA_TOOL_NAMES`.\n- `gateway/platforms/base.py` — `extract_media` (~3358), `MEDIA_DELIVERY_EXTS`\n  (~1401), protected-span masking.\n- `plugins/platforms/telegram/adapter.py` — `send_document` (~5429),\n  `send_image_file`, `send_voice` (dm-topic reply-anchor retry wrapper).\n\n## Relationship to other skills\n\n- Jordan deliverable workflow (see acms-project-operations): single .md handoff\n  in the work folder → summary in chat → MEDIA: line for phone download.\n- `hermes-agent` (bundled, authoritative for config/platform setup) does not\n  cover this delivery path as of 2026-09-26; [Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

Prove the newly deployed budget-gated ACMS dispatch path against the real worker, implement the architecture directions that are now sufficiently clear, perform bounded technology spikes for unresolved agent-state/Jira/external-agent questions, and avoid inventing unresolved customer-product decisions.\n\n---\n\n# 1. Read first\n\nBefore changing code, read the latest versions of:\n\n```text\nrepository AGENTS.md / contributor instructions\nREQUIREMENTS.md\nARCHITECTURE.md\nFUTURE_WORK.md\nrelevant ADRs/TDRs\nFINAL-HANDOFF-CONTINUATION-20260928.md\n2026-09-29-agentifyme-architecture-workbook-merged-rev5.md\n```\n\nDo not redo already-proven VM114 recovery, ORM slices 2–3 promotion, stale-row supersede, budget dispatch code, Attention UI, or SSE.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Verified baseline\n\nExpected starting state:\n\n```text\nVM114:\n  SSH operational\n  QGA exec/file channels operational\n  no hard reset performed\n\nLLM Manager prod:\n  ORM slices 2–3 live\n\nServer Manager / ARM:\n  reconciler live\n  5 stale failed provisioning rows SUPERSEDED\n  VM124 remains live worker\n\nACMS:\n  budgets live\n  economics/cost-per-accepted-output live\n  budget execution-check wired into dispatch_service\n  Attention API/UI live\n  SSE semantic-event stream live\n\nVM124:\n  healthy\n  Hermes state sync VERIFIED\n```\n\nVerify health/version before modification.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Human gates — do not infer approval\n\nUnless this execution message explicitly authorizes them, do **not** perform:\n\n```text\ncredential rotation\nGitHub branch-protection setting changes\ngpu_samples pruning/downsampling\nreal PDU power actuation\ndestructive runtime deletion\n```\n\nPrepare exact runbooks/PRs where useful, but leave the final gated action for the human.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Phase A — prove real budget-gated ACMS → Bridge → Hermes dispatch\n\nThis is the exact next action from the previous handoff.\n\n## A1. Configure live Bridge targets\n\nOn CT122:\n\n1. inspect current deployment/env conventions;\n2. set `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON` outside Git;\n3. include only the intended test worker (`acms-worker-001` / VM124);\n4. validate without printing secrets;\n5. restart/redeploy only the required ACMS service;\n6. verify `/health`, `/version`, and existing smoke checks.\n\nDo not put Bridge credentials into Git, logs, screenshots, or handoff.\n\n## A2. Create one bounded real Work Item\n\nUse a harmless deterministic task.\n\nRequirements:\n\n```text\nclear Work ID\nclear acceptance criteria\nsmall soft/hard budget\nno destructive operations\nno sensitive/customer data\nno infrastructure change\n```\n\n## A3. Dispatch through the authoritative path\n\nUse:\n\n```text\nPOST /api/v1/dispatch/work/{work_item_id}\n```\n\nProve:\n\n```text\nWork\n→ budget execution-check\n→ execution task\n→ Bridge\n→ Hermes\n→ model execution\n→ result\n→ handoff/checkpoint\n→ cost attribution\n→ economics records\n→ semantic events\n```\n\n## A4. Validate idempotency/accounting\n\nVerify:\n\n```text\nretry does not create duplicate execution\nmodel/provider/session linkage correct\ntoken/cost records correlated\nnormal successful execution does not create spurious Attention\nSSE reflects meaningful state changes\n```\n\nUse tests/mocks for hard-budget denial if a production denial probe would be wasteful.\n\n## A5. Record proof\n\nCapture:\n\n```text\nWork ID\nAssignment ID\nExecution Session ID\nA2A/Bridge task ID\nmodel/provider\ncost/tokens\nresult\nbudget state\nhandoff reference\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Phase B — codify measurable-goal Work lifecycle\n\nHuman direction from the merged workbook:\n\n```text\nmeasurable goal\n→ execute\n→ handoff\n→ next measurable goal gets a new plan/fresh session\n```\n\nCurrent ACMS may still use multiple execution sessions inside one Work Item when needed.\n\n## B1. Update architecture/requirements\n\nCodify this **default policy** without removing flexibility:\n\n```text\nFeature / Program\n  → Work Item = one measurable goal\n      → one or more execution sessions if needed\n      → complete handoff\n  → next measurable goal = next child Work Item\n```\n\nA session crash, context rotation, or retry during the same goal does **not** automatically require a new Work Item.\n\nIf an ADR decision still needs human acceptance, keep it Proposed and make only additive/non-breaking changes.\n\n## B2. Parent/child continuity\n\nInspect existing hierarchy first.\n\nIf not already supported, add the smallest safe representation needed to link sequential child Work Items to a parent Feature/Program.\n\nDo not build a competing hierarchy.\n\n## B3. Handoff → next work\n\nWhere existing Executive Agent authorization already permits it, support:\n\n```text\ncompleted Work + complete handoff\n→ Executive Agent creates/proposes next child Work\n→ same approved parent scope\n→ provenance recorded\n```\n\nNo silent scope expansion.\n\n## B4. Review-loop guardrail documentation\n\nAdd an architecture rule:\n\n```text\nreview findings = BLOCKING / REQUIRED / OPTIONAL\nOPTIONAL findings do not prevent completion\nreview does not create new product scope\n```\n\nDo not yet invent a hard review-loop count unless repository requirements already define one.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Phase C — recovery learning and anti-infinite-loop behavior\n\nThe human has now given concrete runtime/work retry direction.\n\n## C1. Runtime recovery\n\nPolicy:\n\n```text\nmaximum 5 automatic recovery attempts\nattempts should vary where possible\nthen Human Attention\n```\n\nAudit current ARM reconciler against this.\n\n## C2. Persist recovery-method history\n\nIf missing, record at least:\n\n```text\nfailure class/signature\nattempt number\nrecovery method\ntimestamp\nresult\ntime-to-recovery\nerror\n```\n\nPrefer semantic/audit records over a second mutable history system if the existing event model is sufficient.\n\n## C3. Strategy progression\n\nImplement bounded strategy progression for failure classes actually supported by the current system.\n\nExample only:\n\n```text\nobserve/recheck\n→ restart affected Bridge/harness\n→ restart service/runtime\n→ guest/VM restart\n→ final safe recovery strategy\n→ Human Attention\n```\n\nDo **not** automatically make PDU hard-cycle the fifth step.\n\n## C4. Running without ACMS assignment\n\nHuman direction:\n\n```text\ndo not automatically stop\n```\n\nAdd/confirm an informational/warning semantic state:\n\n```text\nruntime RUNNING\nprimary ACMS assignment absent\n```\n\nNo automatic remediation.\n\n## C5. Work reassignment loop guard\n\nArchitecture direction:\n\n```text\nmaximum 3 failed reassignments by Executive Agent\n→ stop automatic reassignment\n→ prominent Human Attention\n```\n\nAdd data/audit support for:\n\n```text\nfailed_work_count\nreassignment_count\nretry/reassignment reason\nchanged dimension\n```\n\nDo not automate the old subjective `<60% instruction match` rule.\n\nInstead, document that failure should eventually be based on explicit acceptance criteria/evaluation.\n\nRequire a retry to record what changed, such as:\n\n```text\ndifferent agent/model\nrevised instructions\nadditional context\ndifferent strategy\nhuman clarification\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Phase D — enforce Agent-State ownership boundary\n\nHuman direction:\n\n```text\nServer Manager / Agent Runtime Manager owns agent state-sync health\nACMS should not manage implementation details of Git/cloud backup\n```\n\n## D1. Audit current data placement\n\nInspect whether ACMS currently stores/displays:\n\n```text\nlast state commit\nlast state push\nGit branch\nsync health\n```\n\nDistinguish:\n\n```text\noperational state owned by ARM\nvs\nhigh-level agent health/reference that ACMS may consume\n```\n\n## D2. Correct boundary non-destructively\n\nIf ACMS has duplicate authoritative sync state:\n\n- make ARM authoritative;\n- ACMS may consume a summarized health signal if operationally useful;\n- do not delete existing data until compatibility/migration is proven.\n\nDocument ownership in architecture/contracts.\n\n## D3. Local redundancy tech spike\n\nThe human wants a local copy in addition to cloud Git.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/AGENT_STATE_LOCAL_REDUNDANCY_TECH_SPIKE.md\n```\n\nCompare at least:\n\n```text\nbare Git mirror\nForgejo/Gitea\nobject storage snapshots\nfilesystem/ZFS/workspace snapshots\nhybrid approaches\n```\n\nEvaluate:\n\n```text\nrecovery\nversion history\nhuman inspection\nper-agent isolation\nlarge files\nsecrets\nsearch\nmaintenance\nlocal/cloud redundancy\n```\n\nDo not deploy a new service merely because it scores well; make a recommendation.\n\n## D4. Git vs Agent State technology spike\n\nCreate a second or combined spike comparing Git-backed state with a purpose-built durable agent-workspace/state model.\n\nInclude the human's concern that source code and agent memory may deserve separate storage semantics.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Phase E — Jira / ACMS work-management architecture spike\n\nThe human already uses Jira and wants ACMS compatible with Jira-like work management.\n\nDo not implement a connector before deciding authority.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/JIRA_ACMS_INTEGRATION_OPTIONS.md\n```\n\nCompare:\n\n## Option A\n```text\nJira authoritative for human planning\nACMS manages AI execution beneath Jira issues\n```\n\n## Option B\n```text\nACMS authoritative\nselected Work/Feature status mirrored to Jira\n```\n\n## Option C\n```text\nbidirectional synchronization\n```\n\n## Option D\n```text\nJira parent issues\nACMS-only AI micro-work\nsummary/progress sync upward\n```\n\nAlso briefly compare Linear only where it materially differs for the intended AI-agent workflow.\n\nEvaluate:\n\n```text\nsource-of-truth conflicts\nID correlation\nstatus mapping\noffline/failure handling\ncomments/handoffs\npermissions\nAPI cost/rate limits\nhuman usability\nimplementation complexity\n```\n\nRecommend one initial integration direction, but do not mutate Jira without explicit authorization.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Phase F — Speed-to-Lead / external-agent architecture discovery\n\nThe human has made several important decisions but explicitly requested a separate architecture session.\n\nDo **not** build the full product.\n\n## F1. Record current decisions\n\nDocument:\n\n```text\nSpeed-to-Lead = separate evolving product/repository\nACMS = internal orchestration infrastructure\nexternal/customer Hermes starts sanitized/blank\ninternal company state must never be inherited\nSpeed-to-Lead is first external agent class\nfirst portal persona = real-estate broker owner/operator\n```\n\n## F2. External agent bootstrap proposal\n\nCreate a proposal for a separate safe baseline, for example:\n\n```text\nagentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nIt may reuse safe generic mechanisms from `hermes-base-setup`:\n\n```text\n.gitignore strategy\nsecret scan\nbootstrap/version metadata\nstate-sync mechanics\n```\n\nIt must not copy:\n\n```text\nStartup Teams internal memories\ninternal skills\ninternal company instructions\ninternal secrets/configuration\n```\n\nDo not create the repo unless repository creation is within existing authorization; otherwise produce the proposed structure.\n\n## F3. Explain/compare “Gateway” alternatives\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/EXTERNAL_AGENT_PRODUCT_BOUNDARY_OPTIONS.md\n```\n\nExplain that a service gateway is more than a firewall.\n\nCompare:\n\n```text\nA. Product Backend/BFF → ACMS adapter\nB. Dedicated External Agent Gateway\nC. Edge API gateway + Speed-to-Lead product service + ACMS adapter\nD. Public multi-tenant ACMS API (likely inconsistent with current direction)\n```\n\nEvaluate where to place:\n\n```text\nauthentication\ntenant identity/isolation\nrate limits\nspend limits\npayload validation\ntool allowlists\ncustomer/client guardrails\nauditing\nA2A adaptation\n```\n\nDo not choose a security boundary merely because it is called a \"gateway.\"\n\n## F4. Threat/assumption analysis\n\nThe human identified the highest-risk technical assumption:\n\n```text\nexternal boundary actually isolates customers\nand enforces adequate guardrails\n```\n\nProduce an evidence-oriented threat model/test plan covering at least:\n\n```text\ncross-tenant data access\ncredential misuse\ntool outside allowlist\nprompt/policy bypass\ndata exfiltration\nrate/spend abuse\nwrong-customer routing\nexternal agent compromise\naudit reconstruction\n```\n\nNo penetration testing against production customer systems.\n\n## F5. A2A placement\n\nEvaluate where A2A belongs:\n\n```text\nACMS ↔ Bridge/Agent\nGateway ↔ Bridge/Agent\nProduct Backend ↔ Gateway\n```\n\nPrefer standards-compatible boundaries but do not force A2A at every layer.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 10. Phase G — portal requirements/mockup discovery\n\nThe human explicitly said portal UX questions should be answered after reviewing existing requirements and mockups.\n\n## G1. Find existing artifacts\n\nSearch the Startup Teams repositories/files available to the agent for:\n\n```text\nagentify.me.co\nAgentifyMe portal\nSpeed-to-Lead\ncustomer portal\nreal estate broker\nUI mockup\nwireframe\nrequirements\n```\n\nDo not invent missing artifacts.\n\n## G2. Produce a discovery handoff\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/AGENTIFYME_PORTAL_REQUIREMENTS_DISCOVERY.md\n```\n\nSummarize:\n\n```text\nartifact/repo\ncurrent personas\ncurrent screens\ntop actions implied\ndata needed\nbackend assumptions\ncontradictions\nmissing decisions\n```\n\n## G3. Do not redesign UI yet\n\nNo major frontend implementation until the human reviews the discovery and confirms the portal architecture.\n\n---\n\n# 11. Phase H — provider-independent design-memory tech spike\n\nHuman direction:\n\n```text\ndo not rely permanently on ChatGPT as the only durable design-memory provider\n```\n\nDo not build a vector DB just because it sounds appropriate.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/PROVIDER_INDEPENDENT_DESIGN_MEMORY_TECH_SPIKE.md\n```\n\nCompare:\n\n```text\nACMS relational records + Markdown artifacts\nobject storage + metadata DB\nGit/Markdown\ndocument DB\nvector index layered over canonical storage\ndedicated Knowledge/Design Memory service\nhybrid approaches\n```\n\nEvaluate:\n\n```text\nhuman readability\nprovider independence\nsearch\nversioning\nprovenance\nretention\ncost\nbackup/restore\ntenant isolation\nability to reconstruct context for another model\n```\n\nTreat vector search as retrieval/indexing unless there is a compelling reason for it to become canonical storage.\n\nRecommend an MVP migration path from the current ChatGPT-heavy workflow.\n\n---\n\n# 12. Phase I — LLM Manager / GPU telemetry work that is safe before the retention decision\n\nDo not prune/downsample `gpu_samples` without human approval.\n\nSafe work:\n\n1. verify the prepared GPU retention proposal remains accurate;\n2. inventory ORM slice 4 tables;\n3. separate work blocked by `gpu_samples` retention from unrelated slice-4 work;\n4. add read-only models/tests where no retention change is required;\n5. plan slices 5–6;\n6. do not remove legacy psycopg2 paths until parity/write migration is proven.\n\nIf the human approves Option A during the session, execute it only with:\n\n```text\naggregation table/migration\nbackfill\naggregation verification\nbackup\nretention config\ndry-run/prune validation\nrollback\nthen pruning\n```\n\nNo destructive cleanup first.\n\n---\n\n# 13. Credential rotation and branch protection\n\nThese remain useful but human-gated.\n\n## Credential rotation\n\nIf explicitly authorized:\n\n- execute the prepared runbook;\n- never print secret values;\n- update consumers atomically;\n- verify new credentials;\n- revoke old credentials;\n- run end-to-end worker check.\n\nIf not authorized, leave as Human Attention.\n\n## Branch protection\n\nIf explicitly authorized:\n\n```text\nPR required\nrequired checks\nenforce for admins\nno routine machine bypass\ndocument emergency path\n```\n\nIf not authorized, leave unchanged.\n\n---\n\n# 14. Update architecture/requirements\n\nCodify this **default policy** without removing flexibility:\n\n```text\nFeature / Program\n  → Work Item = one measurable goal\n      → one or more execution sessions if needed\n      → complete handoff\n  → next measurable goal = next child Work Item\n```\n\nA session crash, context rotation, or retry during the same goal does **not** automatically require a new Work Item.\n\nIf an ADR decision still needs human acceptance, keep it Proposed and make only additive/non-breaking changes.\n\n## B2. Parent/child continuity\n\nInspect existing hierarchy first.\n\nIf not already supported, add the smallest safe representation needed to link sequential child Work Items to a parent Feature/Program.\n\nDo not build a competing hierarchy.\n\n## B3. Handoff → next work\n\nWhere existing Executive Agent authorization already permits it, support:\n\n```text\ncompleted Work + complete handoff\n→ Executive Agent creates/proposes next child Work\n→ same approved parent scope\n→ provenance recorded\n```\n\nNo silent scope expansion.\n\n## B4. Review-loop guardrail documentation\n\nAdd an architecture rule:\n\n```text\nreview findings = BLOCKING / REQUIRED / OPTIONAL\nOPTIONAL findings do not prevent completion\nreview does not create new product scope\n```\n\nDo not yet invent a hard review-loop count unless repository requirements already define one.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Phase C — recovery learning and anti-infinite-loop behavior\n\nThe human has now given concrete runtime/work retry direction.\n\n## C1. Runtime recovery\n\nPolicy:\n\n```text\nmaximum 5 automatic recovery attempts\nattempts should vary where possible\nthen Human Attention\n```\n\nAudit current ARM reconciler against this.\n\n## C2. Persist recovery-method history\n\nIf missing, record at least:\n\n```text\nfailure class/signature\nattempt number\nrecovery method\ntimestamp\nresult\ntime-to-recovery\nerror\n```\n\nPrefer semantic/audit records over a second mutable history system if the existing event model is sufficient.\n\n## C3. Strategy progression\n\nImplement bounded strategy progression for failure classes actually supported by the current system.\n\nExample only:\n\n```text\nobserve/recheck\n→ restart affected Bridge/harness\n→ restart service/runtime\n→ guest/VM restart\n→ final safe recovery strategy\n→ Human Attention\n```\n\nDo **not** automatically make PDU hard-cycle the fifth step.\n\n## C4. Running without ACMS assignment\n\nHuman direction:\n\n```text\ndo not automatically stop\n```\n\nAdd/confirm an informational/warning semantic state:\n\n```text\nruntime RUNNING\nprimary ACMS assignment absent\n```\n\nNo automatic remediation.\n\n## C5. Work reassignment loop guard\n\nArchitecture direction:\n\n```text\nmaximum 3 failed reassignments by Executive Agent\n→ stop automatic reassignment\n→ prominent Human Attention\n```\n\nAdd data/audit support for:\n\n```text\nfailed_work_count\nreassignment_count\nretry/reassignment reason\nchanged dimension\n```\n\nDo not automate the old subjective `<60% instruction match` rule.\n\nInstead, document that failure should eventually be based on explicit acceptance criteria/evaluation.\n\nRequire a retry to record what changed, such as:\n\n```text\ndifferent agent/model\nrevised instructions\nadditional context\ndifferent strategy\nhuman clarification\n```\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Phase D — enforce Agent-State ownership boundary\n\nHuman direction:\n\n```text\nServer Manager / Agent Runtime Manager owns agent state-sync health\nACMS should not manage implementation details of Git/cloud backup\n```\n\n## D1. Audit current data placement\n\nInspect whether ACMS currently stores/displays:\n\n```text\nlast state commit\nlast state push\nGit branch\nsync health\n```\n\nDistinguish:\n\n```text\noperational state owned by ARM\nvs\nhigh-level agent health/reference that ACMS may consume\n```\n\n## D2. Correct boundary non-destructively\n\nIf ACMS has duplicate authoritative sync state:\n\n- make ARM authoritative;\n- ACMS may consume a summarized health signal if operationally useful;\n- do not delete existing data until compatibility/migration is proven.\n\nDocument ownership in architecture/contracts.\n\n## D3. Local redundancy tech spike\n\nThe human wants a local copy in addition to cloud Git.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/AGENT_STATE_LOCAL_REDUNDANCY_TECH_SPIKE.md\n```\n\nCompare at least:\n\n```text\nbare Git mirror\nForgejo/Gitea\nobject storage snapshots\nfilesystem/ZFS/workspace snapshots\nhybrid approaches\n```\n\nEvaluate:\n\n```text\nrecovery\nversion history\nhuman inspection\nper-agent isolation\nlarge files\nsecrets\nsearch\nmaintenance\nlocal/cloud redundancy\n```\n\nDo not deploy a new service merely because it scores well; make a recommendation.\n\n## D4. Git vs Agent State technology spike\n\nCreate a second or combined spike comparing Git-backed state with a purpose-built durable agent-workspace/state model.\n\nInclude the human's concern that source code and agent memory may deserve separate storage semantics.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Phase E — Jira / ACMS work-management architecture spike\n\nThe human already uses Jira and wants ACMS compatible with Jira-like work management.\n\nDo not implement a connector before deciding authority.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\ndocs/architecture/JIRA_ACMS_INTEGRATION_OPTIONS.md\n```\n\nCompare:\n\n## Option A\n```text\nJira authoritative for human planning\nACMS manages AI execution beneath Jira issues\n```\n\n## Option B\n```text\nACMS authoritative\nselected Work/Feature status mirrored to Jira\n```\n\n## Option C\n```text\nbidirectional synchronization\n```\n\n## Option D\n```text\nJira parent issues\nACMS-only AI micro-work\nsummary/progress sync upward\n```\n\nAlso briefly compare Linear only where it materially differs for the intended AI-agent workflow.\n\nEvaluate:\n\n```text\nsource-of-truth conflicts\nID correlation\nstatus mapping\noffline/failure handling\ncomments/handoffs\npermissions\nAPI cost/rate limits\nhuman usability\nimplementation complexity\n```\n\nRecommend one initial integration direction, but do not mutate Jira without explicit authorization.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Phase F — Speed-to-Lead / external-agent architecture discovery\n\nThe human has made several important decisions but explicitly requested a separate architecture session.\n\nDo **not** build the full product.\n\n## F1. Record current decisions\n\nDocument:\n\n```text\nSpeed-to-Lead = separate evolving product/repository\nACMS = internal orchestration infrastructure\nexternal/customer Hermes starts sanitized/blank\ninternal company state must never be inherited\nSpeed-to-Lead is first external agent class\nfirst portal persona = real-estate broker owner/operator\n```\n\n## F2. External agent bootstrap proposal\n\nCreate a proposal for a separate safe baseline, for example:\n\n```text\nagentifyme-external-agent-base\n```\n\nIt may reuse safe generic mechanisms from `hermes-base-setup`:\n\n```text\n.gitignore strategy\nsecret scan\nbootstrap/version metadata\nstate-sync mechanics\n```\n\nIt must not copy:\n\n```text\nStartup Teams internal memories\ninternal skills\ninternal company instructions\ninternal secrets/configuration\n```\n\nDo not create the repo unless repository creation is within existing authorization; otherwise produce the proposed structure.\n\n## F3. Product Backend/BFF → ACMS adapter\nB. Dedicated External Agent Gateway\nC. Edge API gateway + Speed-to-Lead product service + ACMS adapter\nD. Public multi-tenant ACMS API (likely inconsistent with current direction)\n```\n\nEvaluate where to place:\n\n```text\nauthentication\ntenant identity/isolation\nrate limits\nspend limits\npayload validation\ntool allowlists\ncustomer/client guardrails\nauditing\nA2A adaptation\n```\n\nDo not choose a security boundary merely because it is called a \"gateway.\"\n\n## F4. A2A placement\n\nEvaluate where A2A belongs:\n\n```text\nACMS ↔ Bridge/Agent\nGateway ↔ Bridge/Agent\nProduct Backend ↔ Gateway\n```\n\nPrefer standards-compatible boundaries but do not force A2A at every layer.\n\nCheckpoint.\n\n---\n\n# 10. Phase G — portal requirements/mockup discovery\n\nThe human explicitly said portal UX questions should be answered after reviewing existing requirements and mockups.\n\n## G1. Phase I — LLM Manager / GPU telemetry work that is safe before the retention decision\n\nDo not prune/downsample `gpu_samples` without human approval.\n\nSafe work:\n\n1. verify the prepared GPU retention proposal remains accurate;\n2. inventory ORM slice 4 tables;\n3. separate work blocked by `gpu_samples` retention from unrelated slice-4 work;\n4. add read-only models/tests where no retention change is required;\n5. plan slices 5–6;\n6. do not remove legacy psycopg2 paths until parity/write migration is proven.\n\nIf the human approves Option A during the session, execute it only with:\n\n```text\naggregation table/migration\nbackfill\naggregation verification\nbackup\nretention config\ndry-run/prune validation\nrollback\nthen pruning\n```\n\nNo destructive cleanup first.\n\n---\n\n# 13. Credential rotation and branch protection\n\nThese remain useful but human-gated.\n\n## Credential rotation\n\nIf explicitly authorized:\n\n- execute the prepared runbook;\n- never print secret values;\n- update consumers atomically;\n- verify new credentials;\n- revoke old credentials;\n- run end-to-end worker check.\n\nIf not authorized, leave as Human Attention.\n\n## Branch protection\n\nIf explicitly authorized:\n\n```text\nPR required\nrequired checks\nenforce for admins\nno routine machine bypass\ndocument emergency path\n```\n\nIf not authorized, leave unchanged.\n\n---\n\n# 14. # Instructions for AI Agents Working in This Migration Kit

Read `IMPLEMENTATION_INSTRUCTIONS.md` first.

## Non-negotiable safety

- Inspect before changing.
- Do not expose or commit secrets.
- Do not copy runtime/session identity between Hermes instances.
- Protect VM906/STEA-004 as the canonical known-good Hermes baseline.
- Preserve MIAM-00101-specific credentials, `.env`, auth, Docker/runtime/model configuration, and databases.
- Use dry-run/diff before sync or bulk file operations.
- Preserve existing `.gitignore` content; append idempotently.
- Do not directly merge consequential changes without human approval.
- Leave a Markdown handoff with rollback instructions.

## Documentation rule

This package contains navigation/context summaries. Current project repositories and live evidence outrank these summaries when they are newer and authoritative.

The file is also saved at: /home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/cache/documents/doc_cc37ef9e29c0_2026_10_03_Agentifyme_Infrastructure_Feature_Roadmap_next_20_ai.md]\n\n[Content of 2026_10_03_Agentifyme_Infrastructure_Feature_Roadmap_next_20_ai.md]:\n# AgentifyMe Infrastructure Feature Roadmap — Next 20 AI Instruction Files (V2)\n\n**Date:** 2026-10-03  \n**Purpose:** Define the next 20 bounded AI-agent implementation plans after the October 3 Jira-to-outcome proof, using the Jira STNA-86-style structure supplied by Jordan. V2 applies the DKMS repository/deployment/training/trajectory decisions from the latest architecture review.\n\n## Story-point scale\n\n- **0–1:** extremely simple.\n- **2–3:** straightforward, minor complexity.\n- **5:** moderate complexity, bounded problem-solving.\n- **8:** high complexity, significant effort / meaningful roadblocks.\n- **13+:** do not schedule as one instruction file; split first.\n\n## How to interpret the June 2026 sources\n\nThe STNSW-34/37/38/40/41/45 files and role documents are treated as **historical product and infrastructure intent**, not as current implementation truth.\n\nThey remain valuable because they describe:\n\n- employee assistants;\n- business-running/project bots;\n- orchestrator bots;\n- local/cloud model operation;\n- 24/7 availability;\n- backups and remote access;\n- local inference and server infrastructure;\n- GOOD/FAST model/agent metrics;\n- SaaS deployment/hosting;\n- product telemetry and user-feedback loops.\n\nOld model names, provider pricing, hardware assumptions, server names, and dates must be re-verified when a roadmap item is executed.\n\n\n## V2 sequencing decisions\n\n[x] Canonical DKMS repository: `startupteams/dkms-project-framework`.\n\n[x] DKMS deploys on a **new dedicated VM** as a **dedicated Kubernetes service**; it does not run inside ACMS.\n\n[x] DKMS stores selected durable knowledge, lessons, and implementation trajectory rather than detailed ACMS Work statistics.\n\n[x] ACMS and registered STEA agents may submit **Lessons Learned** to DKMS; STEA writes may occur outside Work Items through scoped MCP.\n\n[x] Validated internal failure/recovery lessons may be automatically training-approved, with automatic-vs-human approval and human-review state preserved.\n\n[x] Keep the first ~15 roadmap instruction files predominantly internal infrastructure/reliability/security/evaluation work. External Speed-to-Lead remains late in the sequence.\n\n\n## Default execution pattern\n\n```text\nJira STNA-86-style scope\n→ ACMS Work\n→ Hermes / GLM 5.3 Flash orchestrator\n→ Claude Code / Opus 5.5 medium implementation\n→ tests / deploy / evidence\n→ handoff\n→ DKMS durable summary\n→ Jira review\n```\n\nUse stable agent role names rather than employee names in architecture.\n\n---\n\n# 01. 01-agentifyme-dkms-mvp-ai-agent-instructions.md\n\n**SP:** 8  \n**Title:** AI_AGENT: STEA-004 - Build DKMS Durable Knowledge Management Service MVP  \n**Product:** AgentifyMe Internal AI Infrastructure  \n**Project:** Durable Knowledge Management Service (DKMS)  \n**Sprint:** Roadmap 01\n\n## Problem\n\n- Durable plans, handoffs, architecture trajectory, and failure learning are currently spread across ACMS, Git, Jira, handoff files, and agent state.\n- How do we preserve only high-value knowledge without turning DKMS into a copy of every system?\n- How do we make the knowledge useful to future agents and future training/evaluation while preserving provenance and security?\n\n## Scope\n\n- Create the canonical `startupteams/dkms-project-framework` repository if it is still absent.\n- Provision a **new dedicated DKMS VM**, install the approved lightweight Kubernetes stack, and deploy DKMS + persistent PostgreSQL as a dedicated Kubernetes service.\n- Implement PostgreSQL schema, migrations, Markdown import/export, provenance/versioning, retention class, authority class, and training approval/review metadata.\n- Add internal REST plus narrow `dkms.*` MCP read/write tools.\n- Implement approved-plan and final-handoff ingestion.\n- Implement first failure/recovery/lesson record path, including automatic training approval for validated internal failure/recovery lessons with approval provenance.\n- Deploy internally on the new VM/Kubernetes service and prove PBS + logical database backup/restore plus one live end-to-end Work.\n\n## Output Artifacts\n\n- DKMS repository + architecture docs/ADR\n- PR(s), migrations, tests\n- new dedicated VM + Kubernetes DKMS service\n- MCP tool contract\n- golden-workflow handoff\n\n## Assignee\n\nPrimary today: `AGENT_STEA004_ENTREPRENEUR` / STEA-004 (current executive orchestrator) → Claude Code Opus 5.5 medium. Runtime/VM/node assigned by ACMS/Server Manager at execution; do not hardcode placement.\n\n## Reporter\n\nJordan Ulmer\n\n---\n\n# 02. 02-agentifyme-dkms-ingestion-integrations-ai-agent-instructions.md\n\n**SP:** 8  \n**Title:** AI_AGENT: STEA-004 - Integrate DKMS ingestion with ACMS, Jira, GitHub, and handoffs  \n**Product:** AgentifyMe Internal AI Infrastructure  \n**Project:** DKMS Integrations  \n**Sprint:** Roadmap 02\n\n## Problem\n\n- The DKMS MVP is not valuable if humans/agents must manually copy knowledge into it.\n- Which events deserve durable ingestion and which should remain operational-only?\n- How do retries remain idempotent and source-attributed?\n\n## Scope\n\n- Add asynchronous ingestion for approved instruction plans, final handoffs/summaries, **concise dated Jira/implementation timeline summaries**, selected architecture changes, and selected Git/PR/deployment references.\n- Add an ACMS **Log Lesson Learned** action/API that writes provenance-attributed lessons to DKMS.\n- Support registered STEA direct lesson writes through scoped MCP outside Work Items.\n- Use source UID/version/hash for deduplication.\n- Keep Jira/GitHub authoritative for their native objects and ACMS authoritative for detailed Work statistics.\n- Expose ingestion health/status without blocking normal Work dispatch.\n\n## Output Artifacts\n\n- integration code and tests\n- event/ingestion contracts\n- idempotency evidence\n- operational runbook/handoff\n\n## Assignee\n\nSTEA-004 orchestrator → Claude Code; target domain expertise: program-management/full-stack roles.\n\n## Reporter\n\nJordan Ulmer\n\n---\n\n# 03. 03-agentifyme-dkms-failure-learning-ai-agent-instructions.md\n\n**SP:** 5  \n**Title:** AI_AGENT: STEA-005 - Implement DKMS failure learning and recovery knowledge  \n**Product:** AgentifyMe Internal AI Infrastructure  \n**Project:** Failure Learning  \n**Sprint:** Roadmap 03\n\n## Problem\n\n- Recovery attempts are valuable training and operational data, but repeated agents can forget what already failed.\n- How do we distinguish observation, root-cause inference, validated lesson, and promoted playbook?\n- How can future retries avoid repeating the same failed action?\n\n## Scope\n\n- Create failure/recovery/lesson lifecycle and schemas.\n- Integrate selected ACMS/Server Manager recovery events.\n- Capture failure signature, attempt, result, side effects, time-to-recovery, root cause, prevention, confidence.\n- Provide retrieval by failure class/system and playbook relationship.\n- Seed with sanitized real historical failures and validate one live safe failure.\n\n## Output Artifacts\n\n- failure-learning schema/API\n- recovery-playbook linkage\n- tests + safe failure proof\n- Markdown lesson examples\n- handoff\n\n## Assignee\n\nTarget role: `AGENT_STEA005_SYSTEMS_ENGINEER`; until provisioned, STEA-004 orchestrates Claude Code. Runtime assigned by ACMS.\n\n## Reporter\n\nJordan Ulmer\n\n---\n\n# 04. 04-agentifyme-dkms-context-retrieval-ai-agent-instructions.md\n\n**SP:** 8  \n**Title:** AI_AGENT: STEA-007 - Build DKMS retrieval and minimum context packages  \n**Product:** AgentifyMe Internal AI Infrastructure  \n**Project:** DKMS Retrieval  \n**Sprint:** Roadmap 04\n\n## Problem\n\n- Fresh agents need relevant durable context without reloading enormous histories.\n- Which records are current, authoritative, and relevant to a Work item?\n- How do we avoid stale or untrusted material overriding current scope?\n\n## Scope\n\n- Implement metadata/full-text search and related-record traversal.\n- Add `dkms.context_pack` with selection reasons, freshness, authority, supersession, Work/Jira/project/agent filters.\n- Do not add vector DB yet.\n- Prove fresh-session continuation using plan + latest handoff + relevant failures + decisions.\n\n## Output Artifacts\n\n- retrieval API/MCP tools\n- context-pack schema\n- selection tests\n- fresh-session proof/handoff\n\n## Assignee\n\nTarget role: `AGENT_STEA007_PROGRAM_MANAGEMENT`; [Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

The file is also saved at: /home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/cache/documents/doc_837954a2fd7d_2026_10_02_STEA_004_Jira_Workflow_Recovery_and_Internal_MCP_Gateway.md]\n\n[Content of 2026_10_02_STEA_004_Jira_Workflow_Recovery_and_Internal_MCP_Gateway.md]:\n# STEA-004 / Next Implementation Session\n## Jira -> ACMS -> Agent -> Jira Recovery FIRST, then Internal MCP Gateway Build\n\n**Date:** 2026-10-02  \n**Executor:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur or replacement implementation agent  \n**Primary execution model:** Prefer GPT-5.6 Sol at High/Max reasoning if available  \n**Primary rule:** Do not begin MCP implementation until the existing Jira -> ACMS -> worker -> artifact -> Jira loop is proven working again with STNA-88.\n\n# 0. BLUF\n\nThe ACMS/A2A production path was previously proven end-to-end. Phase A completed backend hygiene, Phase B completed IDs/handoffs/artifacts/repository metrics/power APIs/search, and Phase C completed the operator UI and final production demonstration.\n\nA new real-world test now fails:\n\n```text\nJira STNA-88\nstatus = TO START\nassignee = Startup Teams\nhuman clicks \"Check Jira now\"\nACMS says reconciliation run completed\nBUT:\n  no Work Item appears\n  no worker executes\n  no handoff/artifact appears\n  no Jira execution notes appear\n  requested ACMS version change does not happen\n```\n\nTherefore:\n\n```text\nPRIORITY 0:\nRESTORE AND PROVE JIRA -> ACMS -> AGENT -> JIRA\n```\n\nOnly after that proof passes:\n\n```text\nPRIORITY 1:\nBUILD INTERNAL MCP GATEWAY MVP\n```\n\nApproved MCP shape:\n\n```text\nInternal AI Agent\n      |\n      | MCP\n      v\nhttps://mcp.miam.home.arpa/\n      |\n      +-- acms.*\n      +-- llm.*\n      +-- runtime.*\n      +-- proxmox.*\n      +-- github.*\n      +-- pdu.*\n      +-- power.*\n      +-- jira.*\n      +-- registry.*\n      +-- monitoring.*\n```\n\nThe gateway is one logical endpoint, but not one giant root-access service. Domain services keep authority. Existing REST/A2A/SSE stay in place.\n\n# 1. Source-of-Truth Baseline\n\nVerify live before mutation.\n\nExpected:\n\n```text\nACMS CT122:\n  SHA 7daad53\n  Alembic 0017\n  smoke 12/12\n\nLLM Manager / Server Manager VM114:\n  SHA fce5ba7\n\nFleet:\n  acms-hermes-worker-uid-001\n  acms-hermes-worker-uid-002\n  acms-hermes-worker-uid-003\n  acms-hermes-worker-uid-004\n  acms-hermes-worker-uid-005\n```\n\nDo not rebuild existing proven features:\n\n```text\nWork UIDs\nArtifact UIDs\ncanonical handoffs\nartifact search/detail\nWork Kanban\nagent chat\nInbox\nProducts/Projects\nSlop Ratios\nfacility power/cost UI\nglobal search\nfleet search/sort\n```\n\n# 2. Session Discipline\n\nCreate immediately:\n\n```text\nPLAN-OF-RECORD.md\nSESSION-STATE.md\nSTEER-LOG.md\nROLLBACK.md\nTEST-EVIDENCE.md\n```\n\nAt each checkpoint write:\n\n```text\nDONE\nPARTIAL\nBLOCKED\nLIVE PRODUCTION STATE\nSHAs\nDB MIGRATION STATE\nNEXT 3 ACTIONS\n```\n\nIf model changes mid-session, the new model reads these files and verifies live state before continuing.\n\n# 3. Model Recommendation\n\nDo not rely on a name like \"GPT-5.6 Turbo\" as the canonical OpenAI model identifier.\n\nPreferred for this implementation:\n\n```text\nGPT-5.6 Sol\nreasoning = High or Max\n```\n\nUse plan files, checkpoints, tests, and live evidence as authority. Model choice must not alter requirements.\n\n# 4. STOP GATE\n\nNo MCP code until this passes:\n\n```text\nSTNA-88\n-> ACMS Jira reconcile\n-> Work Item created\n-> Work UID assigned\n-> Project mapped\n-> worker selected\n-> assignment ACK\n-> RUNNING\n-> agent changes ACMS version number\n-> tests\n-> safe deployment\n-> canonical Markdown handoff\n-> Artifact UID\n-> Jira BLUF comment\n-> exact artifact URL\n-> Jira transition through policy\n```\n\nIf this cannot be proven, STOP and write a Jira-workflow diagnostic handoff.\n\n# 5. Window 0 - Diagnose STNA-88\n\nRead STNA-88 directly and record:\n\n```text\nissue key\nproject\nissue type\nassignee account ID/display\nstatus\nlabels\ndescription\ntemplate fields\ncreated/updated\nsprint\nproduct/project fields\n```\n\nFind the exact reconciliation run created by \"Check Jira now\" and record:\n\n```text\nrun ID\nJQL/query\nissues returned\nissues inspected\nissues accepted\nissues rejected\nissues skipped\nissues updated\nerrors\nper-issue reason\n```\n\nA reconcile run must never silently skip a candidate issue.\n\nEvery candidate needs a durable outcome:\n\n```text\nIMPORTED\nALREADY_EXISTS\nNOT_ELIGIBLE\nASSIGNEE_MISMATCH\nSTATUS_MISMATCH\nMISSING_REQUIRED_CONTEXT\nINVALID_TEMPLATE\nPROJECT_MAPPING_FAILED\nERROR\n```\n\nCompare STNA-88 against STNA-87, STNA-86, current Jira SOP, and current config.\n\nInvestigate, do not guess:\n\n```text\nassignee account mismatch\nstatus normalization\nproject/issue-type filters\ntemplate validation\nmissing Product/Project\nlabels\nSprint assumptions\nJQL regression\nTO START normalization\npagination\nADF parsing\ndedupe false positive\nclock/time filter\nstate-machine regression\n```\n\nIf current code cannot explain the skip, add instrumentation first.\n\nThe UI should show, for example:\n\n```text\nRun <id>\nScanned 4\nImported 1\nAlready known 1\nSkipped 2\n\nSTNA-88 -> NOT_ELIGIBLE\nReason: <specific reason>\n```\n\n# 6. Fix Jira Intake\n\nPatch the root cause only.\n\nPreserve:\n\n```text\nmanual Check Jira now\nscheduled poll\nidempotency\none Jira issue -> one Work Item\ndurable existing links\n```\n\nDo not broadly accept every TO START issue.\n\n# 7. STNA-88 Production Acceptance\n\nSTNA-88 is the mandatory proof.\n\nExpected:\n\n```text\nSTNA-88\n-> exactly one ACMS Work Item\n-> ACMS-WORK-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS\n-> Project = ACMS or mapped equivalent\n-> real A2A ACK\n-> actual execution\n```\n\nPrefer worker uid-005 unless scheduler validly chooses another.\n\nAgent must:\n\n```text\nread STNA-88\nfind canonical version source\nchange version correctly\nrun tests\ncommit/PR using repo policy\ndeploy safely\nverify homepage shows new version\n```\n\nDo not fake success by changing display text only if version has a canonical application source.\n\nCompletion creates exactly one canonical handoff artifact:\n\n```text\nACMS-ARTIFACT-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS\n```\n\nJira receives:\n\n```text\nshort BLUF\nWork UID\nArtifact UID\nspecific artifact URL\nproduction proof\n```\n\nTransitions through ACMS policy:\n\n```text\nTO START -> IN PROGRESS -> IN REVIEW\n```\n\nBlocked may transition to BLOCKED.\n\nReviewer/authorized role may later do:\n\n```text\nIN REVIEW -> COMPLETED\n```\n\nTake screenshots/API evidence of the whole path.\n\n# 8. Permanent Jira Regression Tests\n\nAdd golden tests:\n\n```text\ncorrect TO START + assignee imports\nwrong assignee skips with reason\nwrong status skips with reason\nduplicate poll no duplicate Work\ntemplate parsing\nADF description\nmanual Check Jira\nscheduled poll\nJira API failure visible\npagination\nlinked Work reconciliation\nBLOCKED behavior\nDEFERRED behavior\nIN REVIEW no new implementation\nCOMPLETED terminal\n```\n\nAdd UI/history for recent reconciliation runs and per-issue outcomes.\n\n\n# 8A. Amendment Effective

{"output": "acms\nacms.db\nagentifyme_acms.egg-info\nAGENTS.md\nalembic.ini\ncompose.yaml\ndeploy\ndist\ndocs\nFUTURE_WORK.md\nINITIAL_IDEAS.md\nmcp_gateway\nmigrations\npyproject.toml\nREADME.md\nREQUIREMENTS.md\nscripts\nSPRINT.md\ntests\n===\n# AgentifyMe Cloud Management System (ACMS)\n\nACMS is Startup Teams' internal control plane for orchestrating AI agents. It assigns and tracks approved work, preserves context and audit history, supports human steering, and relates agent activity to cost and project progress.\n\nACMS does **not** instantiate AI agents or directly manage model-serving infrastructure. Agent instantiation, model hosting, and inference infrastructure are owned by LLM Manager and related infrastructure services.\n\n## Core principles\n\n- Centralized governance, decentralized execution.\n- One persistent ACMS identity per registered agent.\n- One primary ACMS work assignment per agent, plus zero or more known background routines.\n- Agent2Agent (A2A) is the preferred semantic protocol foundation.\n- A standardized ACMS Agent Bridge adapts Hermes, Pi, Codex, and future harnesses.\n- Significant semantic events are durable; token-level streaming may remain ephemeral.\n- Markdown is the initial durable format for requirements, context, plans, handoffs, and decisions.\n- Human approval is required for scope expansion, protected-context changes, production deployment, credential/permission changes, destructive infrastructure actions, and budget overruns.\n- Internal and external/customer agents are separate trust domains.\n\n## Source of truth\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `REQUIREMENTS.md` | Approved ACMS scope. |\n| `FUTURE_WORK.md` | Deferred/unapproved ACMS capabilities. |\n| `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` | Source-derived research and provenance. |\n| `SPRINT.md` | Current execution plan. |\n| `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md` | Problems ACMS exists to solve. |\n| `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | Architecture intentions and runtime model. |\n| `docs/LLM_MANAGER_DIRECTION.md` | Delegated direction for LLM Manager. |\n| `docs/FIRST_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` | First implementation sequence and PR workflow. |", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

PVE clone API: source = template_node/qemu/{template_vmid},\n        # destination node = target.\n        self._call(\"POST\", f\"/nodes/{spec.template_node}/qemu/{spec.template_vmid}/clone\",\n                   {\"newid\": new_vmid, \"name\": spec.name[:63], \"full\": 1, \"target\": spec.node}, timeout=600)\n\n    def wait_clone_lock_release(self, node: str, vmid: int, max_s: int = 240) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the post-clone lock release.\n\n        During the clone the config read FAILS (PVE 500 'VM is locked (clone)') —\n        a failed read is NOT lock-free; keep waiting (found live: the configure\n        step raced the clone and orphaned a VM).\n        \"\"\"\n        deadline = time.time() + max_s\n        saw_config = False\n        while time.time() < deadline:\n            try:\n                cfg = self._call(\"GET\", f\"/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/config\") or {}\n                saw_config = True\n            except Exception:\n                saw_config = False\n                cfg = {}", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "class ProvisioningRequestRead(BaseModel):\n    id: str\n    agent_id: str\n    request_id: str\n    job_id: str | None\n    runtime_id: str | None\n    state: str\n    authority: str\n    approver: str\n    error: str | None\n\n\nclass CreateAgentRequest(BaseModel):\n    display_name: str = Field(min_length=3, max_length=120)\n    harness: str = \"hermes\"\n    runtime_class: str = \"software_development_worker\"\n    model_route: str | None = None\n    trust_class: TrustClass = TrustClass.INTERNAL\n    authority: str = Field(default=\"human_in_acms\")\n    approver: str = Field(min_length=2, max_length=120)\n    request_id: str | None = None\n\n\nclass CreateAgentResponse(BaseModel):\n    agent_id: str\n    external_registration_id: str\n    request: ProvisioningRequestRead\n\n\ndef _client() -> ServerManagerClient:\n    s = get_settings()\n    if not s.server_manager_base_url or not s.server_manager_token:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=\"Server Manager integration not configured\")\n    return ServerManagerClient(s.server_manager_base_url, s.server_manager_token)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------- routes\n@router.post(\"/agents\", status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED,\n             dependencies=[Depends(require_admin_token)])\nasync def create_agent(body: CreateAgentRequest, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)) -> CreateAgentResponse:\n    \"\"\"Reserve persistent identity + record the approved provisioning request.\n\n    Authority (§14): the caller names where approval came from. The sprint\n    execution context is a valid authority when pre-authorized (REV4 §1/§14).\n    \"\"\"\n    request_id = body.request_id or f\"acms-{uuid.uuid4()}\"\n    # idempotency: same request_id -> the original request\n    existing = (await db.execute(\n        select(ProvisioningRequest).where(ProvisioningRequest.request_id == request_id)\n    )).scalar_one_or_none()\n    if existing is not None:\n        agent = await db.get(AgentRecord, existing.agent_id)\n        return CreateAgentResponse(\n            agent_id=agent.agent_id, external_registration_id=agent.external_registration_id,\n            request=_read(existing))\n\n    agent_id = AgentRecord.new_id()\n    ext_reg = f\"acms-reserved-{agent_id[:8]}\"\n    agent = AgentRecord(\n        agent_id=agent_id, external_registration_id=ext_reg,\n        display_name=body.display_name, trust_class=body.trust_class.value\n        if hasattr(body.trust_class, \"value\") else str(body.trust_class),\n        harness=body.harness, bridge_version=\"pending-bootstrap\",\n        protocol_version=\"acms-bridge-v1\", capability_json=\"{}\",\n        created_at=AgentRecord.now(), updated_at=AgentRecord.now(),\n    )\n    db.add(agent)\n    prov = ProvisioningRequest(\n        id=str(uuid.uuid4()), agent_id=agent_id, request_id=request_id,\n        state=\"APPROVED\", authority=body.authority, approver=body.approver,\n    )\n    db.add(prov)\n    await db.commit()\n    await db.refresh(prov)\n    return CreateAgentResponse(agent_id=agent_id, external_registration_id=ext_reg, request=_read(prov))\n\n\n@router.post(\"/requests/{prov_id}/provision\", status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED,\n             dependencies=[Depends(require_admin_token)])", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "File format (0600, outside Git):\n        # service=token:scope1,scope2\n        svc-acms=token1:runtime:read,runtime:write,job:read,job:submit\n        svc-server-manager=token2:ALL\n    \"\"\"\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    out: dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]] = {}\n    try:\n        with open(path) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line or line.startswith(\"#\") or \"=\" not in line:\n                    continue\n                service, rest = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n                token, _, scope_s = rest.partition(\":\")\n                token = token.strip()\n                if not token:\n                    continue\n                if scope_s.strip() in (\"\", \"ALL\"):\n                    scopes = frozenset(ALL_SCOPES)\n                else:\n                    scopes = frozenset(x.strip() for x in scope_s.split(\",\") if x.strip())\n                out[token] = (service.strip(), scopes)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n    return out\n\n\n@lru_cache(maxsize=1)\ndef _identities_cached(mtime: float) -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    return _load_identities()\n\n\ndef _identities() -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    try:\n        mtime = os.stat(path).st_mtime\n    except OSError:\n        mtime = 0\n    return _identities_cached(mtime)\n\n\ndef constant_time_lookup(token: str) -> ServiceIdentity | None:\n    identities = _identities()\n    for tok, (service, scopes) in identities.items():\n        if hmac.compare_digest(tok, token):\n            return ServiceIdentity(service=service, scopes=scopes)\n    return None\n\n\ndef require_identity(request: Request, scope: str) -> ServiceIdentity:\n    \"\"\"FastAPI dependency: verify bearer token + scope; 401 first, then 403.\"\"\"\n    auth = request.headers.get(\"Authorization\", \"\")\n    if not auth.startswith(\"Bearer \"):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"missing bearer credential\")\n    token = auth[len(\"Bearer \"):].strip()\n    ident = constant_time_lookup(token)\n    if ident is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"invalid credential\")", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
